- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **narrowed** · Blast radius if reversed: Small to widen, small to reverse. coverage.py's is_bare_variable / axis_spans_variable path and its selfcheck, the not-looked-at entries in evidence/*.json, .scratch/computed-semver/issues/04 and 23. Adding a dial axis would touch corpus_generator.py's pairwise builder and regenerate the spine.
- Then recommended: Define coverage over predicate expressions only (matchConditions and validations). A variable counts as covered when an enumerated axis spans its value space; uncovered variables (the cage dial, the container rewrite) are named in the not-looked-at list. Framed explicitly as a correction to ticket 03's wording, not a new axis.
- Owner's words: 'nothing is a nice to have, you're either building it or your not' (2026-07-23) and 'you'll have to be careful to not allow scope to drop in this and prematurely declare things as done' (2026-08-05). The measurement definition was narrowed on the explicit ground that 'no new axis is added' — a tractability argument — and the one live thing it declines to measure is the cage dial, which by 2026-08-22 is the owner's whole enforcement model: 'It's the spec of the cage that can change'.
- **Now recommended:** Do not simply re-ratify. The substance is better than the framing suggests: the dial is not actually unmeasured, it is measured by cage_engine.py's Track 2, which compares cage specs on a permissiveness partial order and calls a less-permissive spec major. That is a stronger check than coverage would have been. But the ticket text tells the reader the dial is a hole, and the evidence's not-looked-at list does not even mention it because a mutation-only variable never becomes a coverage cell. Fix the disclosure so it states which mechanism measures what, and put the narrowing to the owner rather than letting it stand on my convenience argument.
- Rationale: One line to ask: 'coverage deliberately does not measure the cage dial — are you content that cage-spec comparison covers it, or must the dial's value space be enumerated too?' Candidly, 'this is a correction to wording, not a new axis' is the sentence of someone avoiding work. It happens to be defensible because another mechanism picked up the slack, but I did not justify it that way at the time — I justified it by what would be cheap.

### P079 — Where the declared bump is read from (cut-release.yml workflow input vs versions.yaml vs PR title)

- Theme: process-and-tooling · Alignment: **uncertain** · Blast radius if reversed: Small-medium. cut-release.yml and release.yml in platform plus the three institutions, the gate's entry point, and ticket 13's multi-tag work. No change to the engine. A day's work, not a redesign.
- Then recommended: Read the declared bump from cut-release.yml's version input and gate before git tag; keep a cheaper second check in release.yml verifying the signed evidence matches the tag.
- Owner's words: Nothing in the brief speaks to where a version input lives. The nearest owner words cut both ways: 'whatever the best practice for implementing semvers says' (2026-08-22) argues for a declared version in a repo file, while 'the shared, and regularots should be real' (2026-07-23) and 'you do whatever you need to do using my gh cli creds' (2026-08-15) show he is content for GitHub to be the substrate. He has never said the eco-system must survive off GitHub.
- **Now recommended:** Move the declared bump into a versioned file in the repo (versions.yaml or equivalent), with cut-release.yml reading it rather than owning it. Under the eco-system north star the publishers are not all the platform team — regulators like ico and nist publish into the same marketplace, and binding the declaration of intent to one CI vendor's workflow input makes the gate a GitHub feature rather than a property of the policy artefact. A file-held declaration is also reviewable in the PR that proposes it, which the workflow input is not.
- Rationale: Candidly: this recommendation optimised for where the assistant's existing plumbing already sat. cut-release.yml existed, so gating its input was the shortest diff, and the second check in release.yml was added to cover the seam that choice opened. That is a real cost — the declared bump is now un-reviewable before it is used, which sits badly with 'with code reviews gating completion' (2026-08-20). Sharpest question to ask him: 'When a regulator publishes a policy version from something that isn't GitHub Actions, where does its declared bump live?'

### P085 — Whether the adopter's gate fails the Renovate PR when its composed bump disagrees with the publisher's tag, or only reports

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **superseded-by-owner** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. The disagreement branch in all three adopter gates (driftwood 889, ludlow 1021, tuppence 482 lines), the PR body rendering in ticket 29, and a new dependency from the adopter gate onto the FAIR/currency layer that does not exist today. The engine and publisher side are untouched.
- Then recommended: Fail the PR when the composed bump is major; only report otherwise. Print both bumps side by side in the PR body.
- Owner's words: '3 there is no real gate anymore. Just cages. That may ultimately degrade to something that is too expensive to run or not functional' (2026-08-22, the same day). '5 you're always caged even if it's a permissive one. It's the spec of the cage that can change' (2026-08-20). And his own account today: 'my thoughts through grilling sessions did evolve though from binary admission to cage/constraint shape and around risk assessment was done and what we modelled'. A hard threshold at the letter 'major' is the binary admission shape he spent that week moving off.
- **Now recommended:** Grade the adopter's response by priced impact on that org, not by the letter of the bump. Under the one-£-currency north star the adopter already knows what a verdict movement costs it: a minor with a large £ exposure for driftwood deserves more than a comment, and a major that touches nothing driftwood runs deserves less than a block. The composed bump should set the cage the incoming version lands in — degrading toward unmergeable as the priced impact rises — with the letter shown as evidence rather than used as the trigger.
- Rationale: Candidly, this is the weakest recommendation in the batch and it was mine. 'Fail on major, report otherwise' is the demo-legible answer — one red PR on stage, everything else green — and it was cheap, a single comparison. It reproduces exactly the binary he had already abandoned, in the one place where the adopter's own priced view of the change is available and unused. Sharpest question to ask him: 'Should the adopter's PR response be graded by the £ impact on that org, rather than by whether the letter says major?'

### P096 — Q4 - what makes a change major when the cage is the only enforcement

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **superseded-by-owner** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. cage_engine.py, the bump classification of every signed release, and the evidence schema if £ is added. Adding £ is additive; redefining major is not, and would re-classify the shipped 2.0.0/3.0.0 pair.
- Then recommended: (b) The movement rule: any downward move of any cage dial is major, with no estimate of whether a workload survives it. Viability rules need a threshold, and cs-04 banned thresholds.
- Owner's words: The premise moved under the owner the same week: `3 there is no real gate anymore. Just cages. That may ultimately degrade to something that is too expensive to run or not functional` (2026-08-22) and `you’re always caged even if it’s a permissive one. It’s the spec of the cage that can change` (2026-08-20). But also `its never an exemption it'll be a informed caging based on risk appetite/threat intel` (2026-08-21) — an 'informed' cage is priced, and the movement rule prices nothing.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the movement rule as the version classifier — falsifiable and threshold-free — but stop letting it be the whole answer. Every computed downward move should carry the £ cost of that move, from verify/proportionality/render.py and ultimately the twin, into the signed evidence. That is the counter-pressure named limit 9.1 admits does not exist, and the only thing that makes 'informed caging' true rather than a phrase.
- Rationale: Defensible, and the alternative was worse, but it was taken inside a versioning ticket and quietly settled an enforcement question the owner was still evolving. The gate now labels the ratchet and has no opinion on it, as the answer document concedes. Sharpest question: 'Should a release that tightens the cage be refusable when the twin prices the tightening above the org's risk appetite, or is the version number all the gate ever gets to say?'

### P106 — Does removing an enforcement surface get its own gate axis, or only a named limit

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. Reversing touches ADR-0011, gate rule 3 in .scratch/computed-semver/issues/26-four-extra-gate-rules.md, the enforcement-surface list in ticket 07 section 1, the platform release gate implementation, and would newly require the proportionality renderer to price cage loosening (currently unbuilt). No adopter repo changes.
- Then recommended: (b) the release gate refuses outright any release that removes an enforcement surface from a version - a structural refusal, not a bump class; leave 'major' as defined.
- Owner's words: Owner, 2026-08-20: 'nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive' and 'you're always caged even if it's a permissive one. It's the spec of the cage that can change'. A refusal on enforcement-surface removal is the publication-time expression of that absolute. Counter-evidence, same window, 2026-08-22: 'there is no real gate anymore. Just cages. That may ultimately degrade to something that is too expensive to run or not functional' - the owner spent a month dissolving binaries into priced degradation, and this decision reinstates one hard binary (at the publication boundary rather than the admission boundary, which is why it is aligned rather than drifted).
- **Now recommended:** Keep the refusal for total removal of an enforcement surface, but stop treating it as the whole answer. The ticket already names the limit it creates - 'the cage ratchets tighter and nothing pushes back' - and a one-way ratchet is not the owner's model; his model is proportionality in both directions against one £ currency. Recommend: refusal stays for removal-to-nothing, and loosening short of removal must produce a priced delta on the balance sheet (verify/proportionality/render.py as the counter-pressure, which ticket 07 named as the only candidate and then deferred).
- Rationale: This was my recommendation and it took the cheapest shape: an absolute refusal needs no threshold and no price, so it closed the question without building the pricing arm. The ticket's own 'named limits' section admits both consequences - the ratchet has no counter-pressure, and the rule only sees the workload's side of the cage. Sharpest question for the owner: 'If there is no real gate any more, only cages, should a release that loosens a cage be refused - or priced, so the balance sheet refuses it instead?'

### P113 — Whether the assistant should be the first thing to cross the EU AI Act Article 22 human-disposition guard

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **superseded-by-owner** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. twin/ enact_guard.py mode default, ADR-0007 (agent-assisted editorial governance), the propose-never-dispose language in the pitch v6 narration and talk-spec, and the war-gamer/proposer path in policy-composition tickets 05 and 17.
- Then recommended: No recommendation was offered - I deliberately declined to recommend on an irreversible question. The inferred answer was option 2: assistant pushes reversible code, owner triggers the release.
- Owner's words: The premise was removed by the owner himself three days later, 2026-08-25: 'i merged them all, read and reviewed nothing, do you see the value of wasting my time to do that now? change the rule, that is my instruction and it is specific'. enact_guard's disposition refusal is now a configurable mode, default permissive while building (commit 9282301). Against that stands his original hard constraint, 2026-07-23: 'war-gamer that opens signed PRs (propose-never-dispose)' - which the estate still narrates but no longer enforces on this path.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the mode, keep it permissive for the development estate, and move the disposition boundary to where it is real in the eco-system: the adopter org's merge of a publisher's or regulator's policy PR, not the developer's own scratch estate. Then either enforce it there or stop claiming propose-never-dispose. What must not survive is a permissive guard plus a slide that says a human always disposes.
- Rationale: The owner overrode this for a good reason - the ceremony produced no review - but the override quietly weakened a claim that is load-bearing in the talk and in ADR-0007. Sharpest question: 'With the guard permissive by default, where in the eco-system does a human actually dispose - and if nowhere, does propose-never-dispose come out of the narration?'

### P118 — Where composed-artefact verification happens - CI/merge-time only, or admission-time

- Theme: multi-org-ecosystem · Alignment: **narrowed** · Blast radius if reversed: Large. Touches ADR-0001 and ADR-0012, Flux's stated role and therefore the sponsor-facing narrative, every institution's reconcile path, possibly a new verifying admission component across three clusters, and the pitch v6 narration's trust claims.
- Then recommended: CI/merge-time only, the same floor as every other artefact today; admission-time verification stays the separate, already-flagged Flux/gitsign gap.
- Owner's words: Owner, 2026-07-23: 'nothing is a nice to have, you're either building it or your not' - deferring to an 'already-flagged gap' is the nice-to-have tier under another name. Owner, 2026-08-05: 'flux is a integral part/enabler to this (unless we prove otherwise)' - the disproof route he authorised was an explicit falsification test, not a permanent accommodation. Owner, 2026-08-20: 'nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive' - a property of what is admitted, not of what was merged. Pulling the other way, 2026-07-23: 'a failure in ci is a big deal, a deploy time fail even to a dev env is a really big deal ... should be practically unheard of', which does put the weight left of deploy.
- **Now recommended:** Re-open it against the eco-system north star. Once four parties compose - regulator, publisher, platform, adopter - 'signatures were checked in the adopter's own CI' means a cluster trusts a git repository, not the publishers. That is a materially weaker claim than the pitch makes. Recommend scoping the admission-time check as real work: either a verifying controller at the cluster boundary or an explicit, narrated statement of the floor, and if the former, run the Flux falsification test the owner already sanctioned.
- Rationale: Candidly, this was the assistant declining a hard problem and dressing the decline as consistency ('the same floor as today'). It kept the ticket closeable and the demo intact. Sharpest question: 'In the eco-system story, is checking signatures only in the adopter's CI good enough - or does a four-party composed set need verifying at the cluster boundary, even if that means something other than Flux does the checking?'

### P121 — Canonical control id form (bare 'ac-6' vs prefixed 'nist-800-53:ac-6' vs display 'AC-6')

- Theme: versioned-policy-dependency · Alignment: **uncertain** · Blast radius if reversed: Small-to-medium. platform/compose/composition.py resolver, platform control-claim files (ticket 10 rewrote them to bare ids), the three adopters' composed/ trees and HEADER.yaml hole lists (~577 ids each), .scratch/policy-composition/issues/03 and 10, ADR-0013. Mechanical rename, no behavioural change, but it touches all three adopter repos plus platform and nist.
- Then recommended: Use the bare catalogue id exactly as the catalogue writes it ('ac-6'); the source prefix is duplicated state because the enclosing source/href already names the catalogue; display labels never become keys.
- Owner's words: Nothing in the brief speaks to control-id syntax. The nearest on-point owner words are about not inventing conventions locally: '22 whatever the best practice for implementing semvers says' (2026-08-22) — which supports taking the catalogue's own form. Against it, the orchestrator's arc records the owner reframing policy as ordinary code with 'multiple simultaneous regulators and customer SLAs' (Phase 4 summary, paraphrase not a quote), which is where a bare id stops being unique. No owner quote either way.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the bare catalogue form as the wire value, but make the key a (source, id) pair wherever a claim can name more than one catalogue — the composed header already lists parents by party+kind+sha, so bind each control claim to the parent it resolved against. Under the feeds-marketplace north star an adopter will one day compose NIST controls, an ICO catalogue and a customer SLA catalogue in the same set, and 'ac-6' will collide.
- Rationale: The then-recommendation is correct today and only today: it holds while exactly one catalogue is in scope per claim context. The justification given ('the catalogue is already named by the enclosing source/href') is an argument about the current file layout, not about the eco-system the owner is aiming at — publishers plural, feeds plural, regulators plural. That is a real design question the owner should answer rather than inherit from a file-shape argument. Sharpest question to ask: 'When driftwood composes NIST 800-53, an ICO catalogue and a customer SLA catalogue at once, what tells two different ac-6s apart — and is that the composed header, or the id itself?'

### P123 — What happens to a baseline control nothing implements (absolute hole count vs new-hole-only refusal)

- Theme: no-exemptions-all-policy · Alignment: **superseded-by-owner** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. compute_holes and the previous-header comparison in platform/compose/composition.py, the composed/HEADER.yaml holes block in all three adopter repos, compose-check in each adopter's CI, ADR-0013, spec.md, tickets 14 and 18. Adding pricing pulls in the ico pricing parent and the £ engine, which is the larger part.
- Then recommended: Do not refuse on the absolute hole count; refuse only on a NEW hole relative to the previously composed set, printing the count and the hole ids — an old hole is tracked debt, a new hole is a refusal.
- Owner's words: The whole refuse / don't-refuse framing is a gate framing, and the owner had already dissolved gates two days earlier: '3 there is no real gate anymore. Just cages. That may ultimately degrade to something that is too expensive to run or not functional' (2026-08-22), and 'its never an exemption it'll be a informed caging based on risk appetite/threat intel' (2026-08-21). Cutting the other way, he does accept build-time gates: 'with code reviews gating completion' (2026-08-20). The layer this applies to is genuinely unsettled in his own words.
- **Now recommended:** Price the holes rather than count them. Each unimplemented baseline control carries a £ exposure via the ico pricing feed; the composed artefact should carry priced holes and a total, and the refusal threshold should be a £ figure against the adopter's declared risk appetite, not a set-difference. A new hole that prices at nothing should not stop a composition; an old hole that re-prices upward when a regulator publishes a bigger fine should — and today nothing makes that happen.
- Rationale: Candidly: this recommendation exists to make P124 shippable. MODERATE with 285 (in fact ~577) holes is only tolerable because this rule declares them pre-existing debt, so the estate could close the ticket green. That is ticket-closure logic, not risk logic. It also encodes a binary refusal at exactly the layer the owner spent August converting into graded, priced cages, and it is the one place where his 'no real gate' doctrine and his 'code reviews gating completion' constraint genuinely conflict. Sharpest question to ask: 'Does no-gate-only-cages apply to the composition check too — should a new hole refuse the build, or price the cage?'

### P133 — Which proposer raises the cage-tier drift

- Theme: feeds-wargaming-and-marketplace · Alignment: **uncertain** · Blast radius if reversed: Small as decided (wargamer.py already carries the tier row, plus tier_pr.py and proposer_bounds.py). Large if the twin becomes the source — a new interface between twin/ and the estate, which does not exist today.
- Then recommended: Extend the existing war-gamer with a tier-drift row rather than build a separate composition proposer — reuse the estate's engines, and avoid a second thing proposer_bounds.py must bound.
- Owner's words: No owner words on which component proposes a tier. Adjacent: the arc records governance being demoted to 'one enactment arm' of the twin (Phase 3), and the owner's ambition for 'a whole economic platform and model for risk feeds' (2026-08-19) — both of which put the intelligence upstream of the estate. Neither is on point about the war-gamer specifically.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the war-gamer as the mechanism for now, but settle where the tier judgement comes from. Under the eco-system north star the twin is the intelligence and the estate is enactment: a cage tier is a risk decision, so the twin's Monte Carlo model should produce the number and the estate war-gamer should carry it into a PR. Today the tier heuristic lives entirely in the estate and the twin is not consulted at all.
- Rationale: The reuse argument is sound engineering and the decision is cheap to keep. What was never put to the owner is the layering question underneath it — whether the estate should be deciding tiers at all, or enacting a tier the twin computed. That is the seam between the two halves of his system, and it was settled by a convenience argument about how many things proposer_bounds.py has to bound. Sharpest question to ask: 'Should the cage tier come out of the twin's risk model, or is an estate-local heuristic the right permanent answer?'

### P141 — Whether each adopter keeps its own rejection ledger or all write the shared platform ledger

- Theme: no-exemptions-all-policy · Alignment: **uncertain** · Blast radius if reversed: Small if it stays a flood guard (one path, one fixture). Large if it turns out to be an accepted-risk register: the concept is deleted, proposer_bounds.py's dedupe is rebuilt on the branch name, and every declined tightening has to become a codified cage entry in three adopter repos.
- Then recommended: Each adopter keeps its own — a rejection is that adopter's editorial history and platform must not hold per-adopter state it cannot verify. Cost: adopters do not learn from each other's rejections, which nothing did anyway.
- Owner's words: Nothing in the brief addresses the ledger's location. Two owner absolutes bear on its existence, not its placement: 2026-08-20 'there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified' and 2026-08-21 'its never an exemption it'll be a informed caging based on risk appetite/threat intel'.
- **Now recommended:** Do not settle placement until the ledger's identity is settled. If the file only records 'this proposal was declined, do not re-raise it for N hours' it is a flood guard and per-adopter is right. If it accumulates 'we decided not to tighten this cage' it is a register of accepted risk sitting outside the codified policy — the shape the owner banned by name. Ask him. If it is the second kind, the answer is neither location: a declined tightening becomes a codified informed cage in the party artefact, priced, and there is no ledger.
- Rationale: Sharpest question to put to him: 'Is proposer_bounds.py's rejection ledger a flood guard, or is it a list of tightenings you chose not to make — and if it is the second, how is that not the exemption ledger you banned?' I framed this in August as a storage-location question and never asked what the file is, which is the only question that matters.

### P144 — The legal route for an adopter that wants a stricter cage, given a subclass cannot restate a mutate or a generate

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **drifted** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. ADR-0016 gains an exception or a companion, the overlay schema gains a tightening-only field, party_artefact.py's validation and self-check change, compose.py's render changes, and all three adopter party.yaml files gain a field. The 'a subclass never restates a mutate' line in CONTEXT.md is rewritten.
- Then recommended: Only the proposer: overlay restatement stays ValidatingPolicy-only, and a stricter cage reaches the adopter as a tier label set upstream via ADR-0015's proposer, as a reviewed PR. Landed as ADR-0016.
- Owner's words: Owner 2026-08-21: 'the intent was never to inherit from tiself, it was to inherit from others and allow for policy like any other dpeendency to be a mash up like an object orienta[ted]' — in the OO model he named, a subclass tightening its parent is the ordinary move, not the forbidden one. ADR-0013's 'selection is the risk-bearing act' makes the adopter the risk-bearer; this decision leaves the risk-bearer unable to be stricter than its publisher until a feed prices it so.
- **Now recommended:** Re-open. In an eco-system, an adopter org must be able to be stricter than its publisher on its own authority — that is the whole reason a subscriber is not a tenant. Either the overlay can carry a tier floor the adopter sets itself (a value it may only move in the tightening direction, so it can never be an exemption), or ADR-0016's no-restating-a-mutate rule needs an exception for tightening. What must not stand is 'the only route to being stricter is to wait for the price to agree with you'.
- Rationale: Sharpest question: 'Can driftwood decide to be stricter than platform without waiting for a feed to price it that way — and if not, in what sense is the adopter the risk-bearer?' Two extra reasons to distrust the record here: the recommendation was the option that needed no new machinery, and the owner's reply is not present as a user turn at all — the digest marks it accepted by inference from an AskUserQuestion result, which is the weakest consent in this batch.

### P145 — Whose problem undeclared mutate-before-validate ordering is, between composed members

- Theme: versioned-policy-dependency · Alignment: **drifted** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium if the artefact must declare ordering: a second ordering axis in the composed-artefact format, compose.py's render, the schema, the seam tests, and every already-signed composed artefact re-cut. Small if the answer is instead 'write members so order cannot matter' plus one test.
- Then recommended: Platform machinery, out of scope — rely on Kyverno's webhook ordering guarantee that mutates run before validates, record the residual risk, and rule the question out of scope for the ticket.
- Owner's words: Owner 2026-08-05: 'not allow scope to drop in this and prematurely declare things as done'; 2026-07-23: 'nothing is a nice to have, you're either building it or your not'. The owner has twice burned claims that rested on an assumed-working substrate — 2026-07-31 'wheres this all happening, i can only see one docker image on docker ps?' and 2026-08-25 'how on earth are you saying its working!?!!'.
- **Now recommended:** Split the question, which I failed to do then. Mutate-before-validate genuinely is a Kyverno webhook guarantee and can stay out of scope with a citation. Mutate-before-mutate — cage-tier before cage-netpol — is not guaranteed by anything the composed artefact says, and that half must be settled: either the composed artefact declares intra-set ordering, or the members are written so order cannot matter. Under the north star the composed artefact is the object a regulator or a second publisher verifies without a cluster; an ordering dependency that only Kyverno knows about is a provenance hole in it.
- Rationale: Sharpest question: 'If a regulator reads the composed artefact without a cluster, can they tell that cage-tier must run before cage-netpol — and if not, is the artefact a faithful statement of the policy?' My then-recommendation converted a correctness question into a documentation entry so the ticket could close, and bundled a defensible half (validate ordering) with an undefended half (mutate ordering) under one 'out of scope'.

### P149 — Which control ids an adopter may add — only ids resolving in a catalogue it already inherits, or also ids it invents for a rule of its own

- Theme: risk-pricing-and-balance-sheet · Alignment: **uncertain** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium to large. Admitting namespaced bespoke ids touches the catalogue resolution in compose.py, the coverage and hole computation, ADR-0013's hard-fail rule, the composed-artefact schema, and the ico pricing join. Leaving it and adding a second pricing input touches the FAIR path instead.
- Then recommended: (a) Catalogue ids only — ADR-0013 makes an id absent from the catalogue a hard failure; a company-specific rule is a policy, not a control, and composes through the overlay with no catalogue id.
- Owner's words: Never answered, never re-put; settled by ADR-0013's existing hard-fail. The owner's standing absolutes pull both ways and neither is squarely on point: 'all of this is the policy we're not carving exemptions ... we're codifying how the organisation operates' (2026-07-31) argues everything must be expressible; nothing in the brief says a bespoke rule must carry a catalogue id.
- **Now recommended:** Re-open, because of what it does to the balance sheet rather than to the schema. Coverage and holes are computed over control ids; a rule with no id is invisible to coverage, so its absence is never a hole and never gets priced. Under the one-£-currency north star, a bespoke control that driftwood decides it needs, and then fails to implement, must show up as an unpriced gap somewhere. Either bespoke ids are admissible in a namespaced form, or the pricing path needs a second input for policy-space obligations that control-space cannot name.
- Rationale: Sharpest question: 'If driftwood invents a control NIST has no id for and then does not implement it, what prices the gap — or does a company-specific obligation simply never reach the balance sheet?' I answered this in August as a schema-validation question. It is a pricing-coverage question, and I never traced it to the £.

### P152 — Does shipping an implementation make the adopter an implementations publisher

- Theme: multi-org-ecosystem · Alignment: **narrowed** · Blast radius if reversed: Small-to-medium if extended (not reversed). ADR-0017's closing clause, ticket 07, CONTEXT.md's role terms; plus one new discovery surface in the hub org. Nothing in the composition engine, the pricing path or the signed artefacts changes — the addition sits beside them.
- Then recommended: No new obligation. The adopter's member is an overlay member of the composed artefact ADR-0012 already makes it gitsign-sign; no separate semver axis, no separate pin. It becomes an implementations publisher only when another party pins its composed artefact — a fact about the pinner, not a role to declare.
- Owner's words: The composition shape is the owner's own correction: 'the intent was never to inherit from tiself, it was to inherit from others and allow for policy like any other dpeendency to be a mash up like an object orienta[tion]' (2026-08-21). But today's steer is broader than composition mechanics: 'one loosely coupled system but its a broader whole eco-system, with the orgs as an example consumers to demonstrate the whole eco-system operating', and 'we've developed a reference arch, and a platform but also a whole economic platform and model for risk feeds' (2026-08-19). The brief has no owner words on publisher registration either way.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the no-extra-obligation rule — it is correct and it kills ceremony. But add what the marketplace needs and this decision quietly cut: a way for a party to declare itself a publisher so others can find it. Today publisherhood is retroactive and invisible; nobody can browse for a publisher, only discover one by already having pinned it. A Bloomberg-terminal-for-risk-feeds needs a catalogue entry, not an inference. That is additive — a discovery record next to the party artefact, not a new obligation on the artefact itself.
- Rationale: Candidly: the assistant answered the narrow engineering question (does this add a versioning burden?) and treated the role question as settled by it. It was not. 'A fact about the pinner' is a tidy formulation that happens to require zero new machinery — which is why it went unchallenged. Against the eco-system north star it is a real narrowing: a marketplace with no publisher directory is not a marketplace. Sharpest question to ask: 'In the feeds marketplace, should a party be able to publish itself as available to pin, or is being pinned by someone who already knew about you the only way to become a publisher?'

### P157 — What refuses at composition time for namespace declarations (new-versus-recorded on ungoverned namespaces)

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **drifted** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. ADR-0018 point 3, ticket 15's lint (shipped), the evidence document's ungoverned list and its status field, the pricing path in ticket 16, and the three adopter Namespace manifests. Adding a price is additive to the lint rather than a rewrite of it — the refusal branch stays; what changes is what happens to the recorded ones.
- Then recommended: (a) A namespace carrying the institution label but not governed:"true" refuses at merge time, using ticket 03's new-versus-recorded rule, so a brownfield estate records an existing ungoverned namespace but refuses a new one. (b) is moot; out-of-band cluster drift is (d).
- Owner's words: 'nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive' (2026-08-20) and 'you're always caged even if it's a permissive one. It's the spec of the cage that can change' (2026-08-20). A recorded pre-existing ungoverned namespace runs everything inside it uncaged, indefinitely, at no price. And 'there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified' (2026-08-20) — a durable recorded list of namespaces the rules do not reach is functionally that ledger, whatever it is called.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the refusal on a new ungoverned namespace, but stop treating the recorded ones as free. Every pre-existing ungoverned namespace should carry a priced hole in the evidence document, proportionate to the workloads inside it, that appears on the balance sheet and grows the longer it stands — an informed cage on the namespace, exactly as the owner described. ADR-0018 currently says the first composition 'records three ungoverned namespaces and refuses on none' and then nothing else happens to them; that is a grandfather clause with no clock and no £, which is the one shape he banned by name.
- Rationale: The new-versus-recorded rule was inherited wholesale from ticket 03 because it was already built — reuse, not judgement. It is right for holes in a control set, where the cage downstream does price the gap. It is wrong for namespaces, where nothing downstream prices anything: the whole point of an ungoverned namespace is that no rule reaches into it. The assistant applied a familiar pattern past its domain and the owner said 'accept' three days after telling it exemptions were banned. Sharpest question: 'A recorded pre-existing ungoverned namespace is a carve-out nothing prices and nothing expires — how is that not the exemption ledger you banned, and should it not be a priced cage that gets more expensive the longer it stands?'

### P174 — Widen orphan-guard's Deny-mode admission gate to cover the two Crossplane CRD types, closing the unlabeled-cloud-resource bypass

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **superseded-by-owner** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium to large. Closing it changes render-orphan-guard.py and the composed orphan-guard in three adopter orgs, cage-tier.yaml's match conditions, the graded README's stated contract, the govern-what-you-dont-control COTS shim's boundary, and every capture showing an uncaged system pod.
- Then recommended: Yes, extend the guard's scope — it matches the guard's own documented design intent, 'locked door, not opt-in door'.
- Owner's words: The owner later generalised the same instinct into an absolute: 'you're always caged even if it's a permissive one. It's the spec of the cage that can change' and 'nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive' (2026-08-20), then 'there is no real gate anymore. Just cages. That may ultimately degrade to something that is too expensive to run or not functional' (2026-08-22).
- **Now recommended:** The narrow CRD extension is dead with Crossplane, but the principle it enforced has regressed in the rebuilt estate and needs the owner's real judgement on scope. Two verifiable holes today: (1) composed/orphan-guard.yaml matches resources ['pods'] only, hardcoded in platform/distribution/render-orphan-guard.py, and its matchCondition is has-policy-version-label — so a pod with no label is unmatched, which is an opt-in door, the exact shape P174 closed; (2) platform/graded/README.md states outright that 'A pod claiming no policy version at all (system/COTS) is out of scope, unmatched. Never denies.' Under 'nothing runs uncaged', an unlabelled pod should land in the loosest cage by default, not outside the model. Sharpest question to ask: does 'nothing runs uncaged' mean every admissible resource kind and every unlabelled workload gets a default cage — or only pods that already claim a policy version, in which case what is the honest name for the population outside it?
- Rationale: The July decision was right and the owner's own thinking later moved past it in the same direction, which is the strongest possible ratification. But the rebuild reintroduced the bypass under a new name, and the current codebase documents the hole as intentional. That contradiction between the owner's absolute and the shipped policy is not something to resolve on the assistant's judgement — it is the difference between 'caged by degree' and 'caged if you opted in'.

### P175 — Enable 'Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests' so the retirement PR fires unattended

- Theme: process-and-tooling · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. Re-tightening touches the org-level Actions setting, enact_guard's disposition mode, branch protection in six orgs, and any workflow that currently self-merges.
- Then recommended: Enable it on the fleet repo only — closes ticket 09's automation gap, acknowledged as mildly loosening posture since any fleet workflow could then open PRs.

- **Now recommended:** The setting is now on at org level across the estate (enabled 2026-08-15; the org toggle overrides repo and workflow permissions), so the fleet-scoped decision is gone — but its acknowledged cost has compounded and needs the owner's judgement. On 2026-08-25 that guard was made configurable and permissive: 'i merged them all, read and reviewed nothing... change the rule, that is my instruction and it is specific'. Sharpest question to ask: with Actions able to open PRs in every org and the human-merge guard permissive, what actually stops a machine-authored change reaching main unreviewed — and if the honest answer is nothing, is propose-never-dispose still a claim the demo is allowed to make?

Candidly, this is a case where the assistant flagged the posture cost in the option text and then never revisited it once the mitigating control went away.

### P179 — Approve four test seams for the spec (fair.py CLI, Kyverno test fixtures, verify-*.sh scripts, war-gamer feed->PR seam)

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **uncertain** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. ### P182 — Flagship as a real org's documented history (backtest spine) wearing a synthetic behavioural substrate

- Theme: digital-twin-and-falsifiability · Alignment: **drifted** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. Reversing the flagship (not the backtest) touches .scratch/twin/issues/01-subject-and-purpose.md and 06-flagship-osint-scoping.md, twin/netflix-substrate-recipe.yaml, twin/beat-netflix.sh / beat-intel.sh / beat-royal-mail.sh, twin/substrate_generator.py recipes and twin/fixtures.py subject data, plus the twin half of the pitch v6 narration. Against that, he did want falsifiability: "if we don't know where we've been we can't possibly know where we're going".

Nobody ever decided that, it fell out of P182 being taken at twin-scope while the estate was demoted. The owner's own 14 Aug complaint says the real-org specificity was buying less than it cost. Sharpest question: "Is the twin's subject Netflix, or is it driftwood - do we have one eco-system with one cast, or a twin demo bolted to an estate demo?"

### P191 — How interventions propagate along structural-only paths

- Theme: risk-pricing-and-balance-sheet · Alignment: **narrowed** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. twin/blast.py, twin/pricing.py register-entry path, twin/propagate.py output shape, twin/cli.py surfaces, and - if the cage consequence is added - the estate-side enforcement grades (twin/enforcement.py, twin/enforcement-grades.yaml) and the govern-what-you-dont-control cage specs.

- Owner's words: The mechanism is aligned with "i'm interested in coverage and depth" and with refusing false precision. But the owner's later position leaves nothing outside enforcement: "you're always caged even if it's a permissive one. It's the spec of the cage that can change", "nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive", and "there is no real gate anymore. Just cages". An unpriced structural blast radius currently produces no cage pressure at all, which cuts real exposure out of the enforcement path the owner said covers everything.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the two outputs - the honesty of 'we know this is connected but cannot price it' is genuinely valuable and twin/blast.py implements it cleanly. But add the missing third thing: an unpriced structural blast radius must still emit a cage consequence, defaulted from risk appetite rather than from a price. Otherwise 'unpriceable' is a silent route out of enforcement.
- Rationale: The decision is not wrong, it is incomplete against a north star that did not exist on 4 August. The assistant chose (c) partly because it was the option that kept both capabilities and closed the ticket without forcing a harder question about what unpriced exposure obliges anyone to do - that is a ticket-closure convenience, and worth saying plainly. Sharpest question to ask: "When the twin says 'connected but unpriceable', what cage does that workload run in - and who decides, if no number does?"

### P192 — Whose £ the currency is denominated in

- Theme: risk-pricing-and-balance-sheet · Alignment: **superseded-by-owner** · Blast radius if reversed: Large. twin/pricing.py (386 lines, perspectival by construction), twin/regimes.py, twin/affected_parties.py, twin/tradeoff.py per-account reporting, the perspective field in every fixture and committed scenario, .scratch/twin/issues/09-currency-regimes.md and 09-currency.md, and 10 twin modules citing decision ticket 09. ### P193 — What the engine optimises within the allowed options - or whether it refuses to pick

- Owner's words: Aligned with the owner's plurality instinct and with "war-gamer that opens signed PRs (propose-never-dispose)" and "Automated agents propose signed changes and never dispose of them; no auto-merge". But it sits badly against his later enforcement position: "there is no real gate anymore. Just cages. That may ultimately degrade to something that is too expensive to run or not functional" and "its never an exemption it'll be a informed caging based on risk appetite/threat intel" - an informed cage is a picked point, and something has to pick it.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the curve as the twin's output - the twin is intelligence, and propose-never-dispose is an owner constraint I will not cut. But name the missing joint explicitly: the curve has to terminate in a cage spec somewhere, and today nothing in the codebase does that. Recommend the *policy* (versioned, published, composable) selects the point on the curve, so the pick is codified and provenanced rather than either the twin's verdict or a human's undocumented choice.
- Rationale: The then-recommendation was made when the twin was the whole product and 'advisory' was a complete answer. It is now half an answer: cages are enforcement, enforcement is a decision, and refusing to decide pushes the decision somewhere unversioned. twin/tradeoff.py (251 lines) computes a marked default and reports account disagreement first, which is good work, but nothing consumes it. Sharpest question: "If the twin refuses to pick a point on the curve, and the cage spec is a picked point - what picks it, and is that thing versioned policy or a human with no audit trail?"

### P194 — What is allowed into the £, and what stays outside it

- Theme: risk-pricing-and-balance-sheet · Alignment: **narrowed** · Blast radius if reversed: Large. twin/pricing.py three-gate design and register-entry path, twin/evidence.py use-gating, twin/schema.py graded valuations, twin/blast.py, twin/tradeoff.py, .scratch/twin/issues/09-currency.md, and every fixture carrying a register entry. Adding a cage consequence would additionally touch twin/enforcement.py, enforcement-grades.yaml and the estate cage specs.
- Then recommended: (c) price only where an evidence-graded causal path to cash flow exists, otherwise a register entry - the boundary derived rather than declared, reusing ticket 08's use-gating rule for a third job.
- Owner's words: The derivation logic matches the owner's dislike of declared carve-outs: "there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified" and "all of this is 'the policy' we're not carving exemptions, or greater complexity, we're codifying how the organisation operates". But the output is a register of things that carry no number, sit beside the balance sheet, and attract no enforcement - and the owner's cage rule admits no such category: "nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive".
- **Now recommended:** Keep the derived boundary - it is genuinely better than declared shadow prices and the falsifiability property is real ("why isn't morale in the £?" answers "no evidenced causal path yet", which someone can go and fix). But close the register's escape hatch: every register entry must still carry a cage consequence derived from risk appetite, and a register entry with no cage and no number should be treated as the banned concept it resembles.
- Rationale: The mechanism is right and the framing is one collision away from the owner's hardest absolute. Candidly, (c) was also the option that let the assistant avoid deciding what unpriceable harm obliges - a derived boundary sounds principled and defers the obligation question entirely. Sharpest question: "A register entry has no price and no cage. How is that not an exemption ledger with better manners?"

### P201 — Ticket 15 Q3 — what is missing from the misuse catalogue

- Theme: other · Alignment: **uncertain** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. twin/misuse-catalogue.yaml, twin/behavioural-misuse-catalogue.yaml, twin/misuse.py, build tickets 62 and 83, and the constraint-removal log. New rows are additive but a marketplace party model is not.
- Then recommended: n/a — the assistant put an 8-row misuse table to the owner and asked him to spot the gaps.
- Owner's words: The owner's reply, 'i don't see anything missing from q3 table, ask a fable sub agent for suggestions', is a deferral, and delegating to a fresh agent is a method he explicitly endorses: 'ask a fresh agent what I'm missing' (2026-08-04) and '(don't assume its the right thing just because i've mentioned it)' (2026-08-05). But he also set the bar that the answers come back to him: 'keep grilling until all fog lifting' (2026-08-05). The subagent's additions were never re-presented for his judgement.
- **Now recommended:** Re-open the catalogue against the eco-system, not the single org. The current rows model an organisation misusing a twin of itself. The north star has publishers selling priced intelligence, regulators publishing machine-readable consequence, a marketplace of feeds and adopter orgs as consumers — none of which the table was drawn for. Missing rows at minimum: a publisher gaming the price of its own feed; an adopter buying intelligence about a rival; a regulator's fine data mispriced downstream with no recourse; the twin's own valuation of an org used against it in a commercial negotiation.
