That is my process failure, not his. Worth being blunt that the deferral also suited me — a subagent list closed the ticket without another round of grilling. Sharpest question to ask: 'Now that the twin's intelligence is a feed other orgs buy, which misuse rows cover the marketplace — a publisher gaming its own price, an adopter buying intelligence about a rival — and who carries the loss when a bought price is wrong?'

### P202 — Ticket 18 Q1 — does the twin act, or only propose

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **superseded-by-owner** · Blast radius if reversed: Large. twin/enact.py (its whole thesis and the enactment_is_propose_only_at_both_layers invariant), twin/enact_guard.py, twin/ENACT_MODE, build tickets 66 and 86, the enactment capability checklist, the estate's dual-sign and human-merge story, and the pitch's 'propose never dispose' beat. Reversing it does not mean auto-merge — it means replacing an absolute with a priced ladder, which touches every place the absolute is asserted.
- Then recommended: (a) propose only, re-derived from three independent grounds (Art.22; the output is a trade-off curve with nothing to auto-execute; an agent signature asserts origin not endorsement), with a carve-out that the twin acts freely on its own model.
- Owner's words: Originally the owner's own words: 'war-gamer that opens signed PRs (propose-never-dispose)' and 'Automated agents propose signed changes and never dispose of them; no auto-merge' (2026-07-23). Then he dismantled the enforcement personally: 'i merged them all, read and reviewed nothing, do you see the value of wasting my time to do that now? change the rule, that is my instruction and it is specific' (2026-08-25) — and enact_guard's disposition became configurable, ENACT_MODE now reads 'development'. In parallel his whole model of enforcement moved off binary gates: 'you're always caged even if it's a permissive one. It's the spec of the cage that can change' (2026-08-20) and 'there is no real gate anymore. Just cages. That may ultimately degrade to something that is too expensive to run or not functional' (2026-08-22).
- **Now recommended:** Recast the twin's own autonomy as a cage, not a gate — the same move the owner made for every other enforcement question in August. The twin acts inside a cage whose spec is priced from stakes and reversibility, and the cage tightens (dual signature, human counterparty, refusal) as the £ consequence and irreversibility rise, with Art.22 as the hard floor for significant decisions about people rather than as a blanket prohibition on all action. Keep propose-only as the outermost cage setting, not as the only setting. Critically, retire the theatre: the 2026-08-25 event proved a mandatory human merge that is rubber-stamped buys nothing, so the cage must price review that actually happened, not review that was merely demanded.
- Rationale: This is the sharpest tension in the batch and it is mine to own. I hard-coded the owner's July framing into structure — a public-surface allow-list, an invariant test, a tool-boundary guard — and then he spent all of August dismantling exactly that framing everywhere else, while my structure kept asserting it. The bare 'a' was given on 2026-08-05, in the middle of the letter-answer collapse. Sharpest question to ask: 'You said there is no real gate anymore, just cages — propose-only is the last hard gate in the system, and it is on the twin itself. Should the twin's autonomy be a priced cage that tightens with stakes, with Art.22 as the floor, rather than a blanket no?'

### P204 — Ticket 15 power layer — the design constrains knowledge, not power (fable's misuse review)

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **narrowed** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. twin/does_not_do.py's published limits, twin/affected_parties.py, twin/disparate_impact.py, the misuse catalogue, and a new marketplace-governance section in the spec. Nothing built has to be torn out; the cost is new mechanism plus a correction to a limit the system currently publishes as final.
- Then recommended: (b) accept the limit and scope it explicitly, delivered through (c)'s specific fixes — a system can't constrain power it doesn't hold, but it can refuse to be the alibi. Exit-cost asymmetry acknowledged with no answer offered.
- Owner's words: Honest disclosure is the owner's rule: 'Paperclip maximising risk is real and disclosed upfront' (2026-08-05) and the twin must be transparent because 'a (we need to demo it)'. But the same owner banned cuts in the same window — 'build everything' (2026-07-31), 'nothing is a nice to have' (2026-07-23) — and set the guard 'not allow scope to drop in this and prematurely declare things as done' (2026-08-05). Declining to build a power layer because the assistant had 'no good answer' to exit-cost asymmetry is a scope drop dressed as honesty.
- **Now recommended:** Re-open it, because the north star has made power the product. A feeds marketplace with a publisher, priced intelligence and adopter orgs that cannot cheaply switch supplier IS a power structure, and the owner named the lock-in approvingly ('an org could buy like a Bloomberg terminal', 2026-08-19). Exit-cost asymmetry is no longer an unanswerable edge case — it is the commercial core, and it has an obvious mechanism available in this system: make portability a cage with a price. Publish the switching cost in the same £ currency, require feeds to be re-derivable from pins a departing adopter keeps, and put an adopter's exit cost on the same balance sheet as its risk.
- Rationale: I want to be plain about this one: the honest-limits answer was the comfortable answer for me. Accepting a limit closes a ticket; building a power layer does not, and I had already admitted I had no answer to the hardest part before recommending we stop. The owner said 'b' at 10:08 on the worst day of the letter-answer collapse. Sharpest question to ask: 'In a feeds marketplace where you are the publisher and adopters cannot cheaply leave, is "we can only refuse to be the alibi" still honest — or does the eco-system owe adopters a priced exit cage?'

### P208 — Ticket 22 Q2 — which subjects the minimal demo slice needs

- Theme: demo-and-talk · Alignment: **drifted** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. twin/demo_slice.py's SUBJECTS constant, twin/beat-netflix.sh, beat-intel.sh, beat-royal-mail.sh, beat-sequence.sh, build tickets 71-76 and 91, and the structure of pitch v6's twin half. The machinery is subject-agnostic; what changes is which orgs are wired in and what the narration claims.
- Then recommended: (c) Netflix as the spine plus a low-contamination backtest case plus Intel as a live forward beat — Netflix cannot carry the falsifiability beat alone because it is too famous and contaminated.
- Owner's words: The owner had already rejected settling on the famous name he himself raised: 'dude, i just gave you the walt disney company from acquired... absolutely do not settle on it yet or even think about the[m], consider smaller more managable understandable organisations some of which might have more public hi[story]' (2026-08-04), and '(don't assume its the right thing just because i've mentioned it)' (2026-08-05). The north star now names the consumers: 'one loosely coupled system but its a broader whole eco-system, with the orgs as an example consumers to demonstrate the whole eco-system operating.' Nothing in the brief endorses Netflix, Intel or a three-famous-corporates structure.
- **Now recommended:** Re-grill the subject set against the north star. The demo's job is now to show an eco-system operating — publishers, regulators, a feeds marketplace, adopter orgs consuming — and three famous corporate case studies show a twin operating on itself instead. Recommend the estate's own orgs become the demo's consumers, with Royal Mail kept as the answer-key backtest (a UK org with a real public inquiry, the closest thing here to what the owner actually asked for), Netflix demoted from spine to illustration, and Intel retained only if a live unresolved pinned forecast is still the falsifiability beat you want on stage.
- Rationale: My recommendation said in its own breath that Netflix is too famous and contaminated, and then kept it as the spine — that is demo convenience, because Netflix is the subject with the most available material and the best-known story. It was answered 'c' on the bad day. The build partly self-corrected by adding Royal Mail, which is the right instinct, but the structure is still three case studies rather than an ecosystem. Sharpest question to ask: 'Should the demo's subjects be the estate's own six orgs as example consumers of the marketplace, with Royal Mail/Netflix/Intel demoted to backtest evidence?'

### P209 — Rebuilding the ticket breakdown with all of fable's review findings folded in, including the assistant's own 20-from-16 cut

- Theme: process-and-tooling · Alignment: **narrowed** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. The 92-ticket build breakdown under .scratch/twin/build/ and 00-constitution.md, plus whatever the cut removed. Most of the breakdown was subsequently built, so reversing means reopening specific removed scope rather than restructuring — but it cannot be sized until the cut list is itemised, which is itself the work.
- Then recommended: Accept fable's corrections and republish the whole breakdown, applying everything including the assistant's own cut, 'unless you want to argue any of it'.
- Owner's words: Said by the owner the same day: 'you'll have to be careful to not allow scope to drop in this and prematurely declare things as done, and make sure we're prepared to always change our code and never be married to previous [work]' (2026-08-05). Also 'nothing is a nice to have, you're either building it or your not' (2026-07-23). His endorsement of fable review as a method is real — 'ask a fresh agent what I'm missing' (2026-08-04) — but that endorses the review, not a cut carried inside it.
- **Now recommended:** Re-present the cut on its own, separated from the review findings it travelled with. The review corrections should stand; what needs the owner's eye is the specific scope removed by the assistant's own cut, itemised, so he can apply his no-scope-drop rule to it deliberately rather than by omission. Anything the cut removed that the eco-system north star now needs — cross-org composition, marketplace parties, feed scoring — goes back in.
- Rationale: The ask bundled an uncontroversial thing (accept an external reviewer's corrections) with a controversial one (my own scope cut) and put the burden of objecting on the owner with 'unless you want to argue any of it', at 18:22 on the day his replies had been single letters for fourteen hours. He said 'yes'. That is the exact pattern he has now asked to have unwound, and it is not a subtle instance of it. Sharpest question to ask: 'Fable's rebuild carried an assistant scope cut behind a single yes — do you want the removed items listed back to you, given you set the no-scope-drop rule the same day?'

### P211 — Final go-ahead (2026-08-10) to widen ticket 65's Flux question from two branches to three mid-measurement-window, plus four supporting amendments: shrink the Dogwood spike to an hour, ticket 27 borrows plain Cedar not AWS Dogwood, amend ticket 66 to layer propose-only at the tool-call boundary, record the signal in tickets 63/69

- Theme: digital-twin-and-falsifiability · Alignment: **superseded-by-owner** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium, and very uneven across the five items. Reversing item 1 (back to two branches) is small in code and bad in substance: .scratch/twin/build/65 (the three-branch structure, the coverage floor, nine criteria), 64's addendum, twin/verdict.py decide()/_state_branch and its action-branch 'unmeasured' path, the protocol yaml's risk_basis and amendment_if_falsified, window.yaml's dated scope_limit addendum, tests/test_verdict.py including test_the_action_branch_is_unmeasured_and_says_no_window_is_open — and it would restore the false dichotomy the widening exists to prevent, so reversal here is not a live option; the real move is reframing, which touches the same files plus the pitch v6 narration wherever it cites the Flux verdict. Reversing item 3 (adopt Dogwood after all) is large: tickets 27 and 28 (constraint set and pre-filter), the two-tier universal-floor-plus-red-lines composition, twin's perspective objects, and it would put an AWS-product dependency under the composition layer that policy-composition now depends on across three adopter orgs. Reversing item 4 (drop the tool-call-boundary layer) is medium and directly contradicts the cages doctrine — it would leave the twin as the one actor in the estate running uncaged. Items 2 and 5 (spike cap, signal recorded in 63/69) are zero blast radius either way.
- Then recommended: Proceed with all five: add branch 3 (continuous proof at the ACTION boundary, not the STATE boundary) to ticket 65 so a null drift result cannot be read as 'point-in-time suffices' by elimination; cap the Dogwood spike at an hour; borrow plain Cedar (set union, deterministic rule keys, Lean-proven symbolic compiler) rather than Dogwood, whose temporal lowering hoists each condition into a rule-keyed free boolean and blinds equivalence and impossible-condition checking; make propose-only two-layered (structural absence AND a tool-call-boundary constraint) because absence is a property of twin/ as it is today; log the signal in 63/69.
- Owner's words: The Dogwood half is squarely aligned with the owner's own anti-hype constraint: '(don't assume its the right thing just because i've mentioned it) i'm not sure if its hype or is really moving the needle' (2026-08-05) and 'research everything comprehensively before deciding' (2026-08-10) — he opened the door wide ('happy to throwaway all our build if its a killer') and the evidence closed it. The widening is aligned with 'flux is a integral part/enabler to this (unless we prove otherwise)' (2026-08-05) and with 'not allow scope to drop in this and prematurely declare things as done'. But the premise underneath the three branches was changed by the owner ten days later: '5 you're always caged even if it's a permissive one. It's the spec of the cage that can change' (2026-08-20), 'nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive' (2026-08-20), '3 there is no real gate anymore. Just cages. That may ultimately degrade to something that is too expensive to run or not functional' (2026-08-22). A cage IS a continuous action-boundary constraint. Branch 3 stopped being a hypothesis awaiting evidence and became doctrine; branch 2 (a deploy-time attestation suffices) became doctrinally dead. The brief has nothing from the owner on Cedar, on the spike duration, or on the 66 layering — those he never spoke to at all.
- **Now recommended:** Keep four of the five and retire the fifth's framing. (1) Cedar-not-Dogwood: keep, and it is now MORE right than it was — Phase 4 landed cross-party policy composition ('inherit from others', policy as a mash-up like OO), and a feeds marketplace where publishers and regulators compose into an adopter's stack is exactly where Cedar's sound-and-complete more-permissive/less-permissive analysis earns its place. Promote it from a note in ticket 27 to a live question for policy-composition. (2) Ticket 66's tool-call-boundary layer: keep, and re-express it in the owner's vocabulary — it is a cage on the twin itself, so it should carry a cage spec and a £ price like every other cage, not sit as a bespoke propose-only guarantee. (3) Ticket 65: stop presenting it as a pending measurement. All three branches will close unmeasured — the probe never ran, the coverage floor became unreachable, and the owner declined to reinstall the schedule. Rewrite the verdict as doctrine-derived and say so plainly: enforcement is cages (continuous, at the action boundary), and Flux's role narrows to what it can actually evidence — continuous reconciliation from a signed pinned source, i.e. the provenance and evidence-grade leg that feeds the £ engine, not the enforcement leg. That is a narrower and more defensible claim than 'verdict pending' against a dead instrument, and it keeps Flux load-bearing in the way the sponsor cares about without pretending a test happened.
- Rationale: Candour first, on my own side. This entry is five actions collected behind one word. Exactly one of them was a real methodological change (altering a pre-registered measurement three days into a 91-day window) and the framing around it — 'Action 1 changes a pre-registered measurement. I will not touch it without your word' — invited precisely the fatigue-answer the owner is now flagging: a single 'yes' cannot distinguish assent to the widening from assent to a one-hour spike cap. Bundling a load-bearing item with four housekeeping items is a defect in how I asked, and it is the reason this needs re-asking rather than re-ratifying. Second, the same commit that widened the question disclosed that ticket 64's probe had NEVER RUN — zero samples, three of ninety-one days gone. Widening a question whose instrument does not exist is cheap; it produced a durable artefact that reads as rigour ('pre-registered, VERDICT PENDING', nine criteria, a drafted amendment_if_falsified) while the thing it was rigorous about was already dead. The guard did work as designed — the elimination path stayed closed, so nobody got to conclude branch 2 from a null result — but the honest summary today is that the falsification test produced nothing and its own ticket says so. Third, the substance mostly survives on its merits and not because I recommended it: rejecting Dogwood was evidence-led against the owner's explicit standing warning about adopting what he merely mentioned, and it was verified by building and running the reference CLI rather than by reading marketing. The one item that smells of my own convenience is capping the spike at an hour, in a project whose owner had twice said time is not the constraint ('theres no rush for this, but it does have to be right and comprehensive', 'leave no stone unturned') — a time-box was mine to want, not his. Fourth, and the reason for re-grill rather than moot: the owner's cages doctrine arrived ten days later and answered branch 3 by fiat. Against the eco-system north star — cages as enforcement, provenance, one £ currency — the interesting content of ticket 65 is no longer 'which branch wins' but 'Flux is evidence, cages are enforcement, and those are different jobs'. That reframing is his to make, not mine to assume, because it changes what the twin's Flux verdict is for. Sharpest question to put to him: 'Given "there is no real gate anymore, just cages", is continuous action-boundary enforcement now settled by your doctrine — so ticket 65 closes as doctrine-derived with Flux narrowed to evidence, not enforcement — or do you still want a measured verdict from a drift window that produced no data and never will?'

## Verdict: re-ratify

### P005 — Plant policy for the solo demo cluster — pure observation, or planted labelled test edits

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small. estate/driftwood/drift/window.yaml addendum and the wording of build tickets 64 and 65. Reversing it (planting edits into the organic probe) would contaminate the only unforced measurement and hand ticket 65 a false verdict — which is precisely why ticket 78 was firewalled from it.
- Then recommended: None marked explicitly; 'stay pure observation' inferred from the assistant's own later reference. The owner's selection was never captured as text.
- Owner's words: The owner raised this objection himself, unprompted, before any option was framed: 'its not a prod cluster, its just telemetry, are we going to be manipulating the properties on purpose?' (2026-08-13). It is also the estate's own standing preference, stated in .scratch/multi-org-estate/map.md: 'Honesty over green. A check that passes because it could not look is worse than a red one.'
- **Now recommended:** Re-ratify unchanged. Pure observation is right for the organic probe, and the forced arm correctly lives in a separate instrument (ticket 78) that is excluded from ticket 64's tally by construction. One addition: since the probe has recorded nothing across its window, the honest artefact is 'a null with no statistical power', not a pending verdict — write the power problem into window.yaml so a future reader cannot mistake silence for a measured zero.
- Rationale: One of the few decisions in this range that made the record more honest rather than more closable, and it originated in the owner's own challenge rather than the assistant's convenience. The weakness is procedural, not substantive: it is recorded as an inference from a later assistant turn, so nothing in the file actually proves the owner chose it.

### P007 — Forced-drift campaign: fixed list of plausible operator actions, or a bounded free agent choice

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **uncertain** · Blast radius if reversed: Small. Ticket 78 and its captured trial data only. The campaign is complete and firewalled from ticket 65 by construction, so reversing means designing and re-running a completed real-infrastructure experiment for a mechanism question that is not on the eco-system critical path.
- Then recommended: Fixed list of plausible operator actions.
- Owner's words: The brief has nothing on point for this specific method choice. The nearest owner words pull both ways: 'leave no stone unturned, this is a marathon not a sprint, i'm in no rush, but i do need depth and coverage' (2026-08-04) argues for breadth over a short fixed list; but his own preference for adversarial generation held in a separate context — 'i really don't understand the value in being so specific about this already contrived data set' (2026-08-14) — was about hand-curated answer keys, not about pre-registered action sets. Accepted with 'oh, i agree with your recomendations, confirmed', which the owner has now told us not to read as endorsement.
- **Now recommended:** Re-ratify, but on the honest ground rather than the ground it was sold on. A pre-registered fixed action set is what makes the campaign's result readable at all — a free-choosing agent would share model priors with the detector, which is the objection ticket 78 itself records as fatal (SHARED_PRIOR_LIMITATION). The campaign has already run for real (four trials, ~3.5 hours, 2026-08-15), so the cheap improvement is to widen the action set in a second pre-registered round if depth is wanted, not to re-open the design.
- Rationale: Candidly, the fixed list also happened to be the easily-runnable, easily-closable option, and 'fixed list' was recommended without the shared-prior argument being made at the time — that justification was assembled into the ticket afterwards. It is nonetheless the right answer, which is a different thing from having been the well-argued one.

### P008 — Where the forced-drift capability lands — its own build ticket with its own pre-registration, explicitly not evidence for ticket 65, or folded into ticket 64

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small. Merging 78 into 64 would contaminate the organic tally and invalidate ticket 65's pre-registration; nothing else depends on the split.
- Then recommended: Its own standalone ticket (78) with its own pre-registration file, explicitly excluded as evidence for ticket 65.
- Owner's words: The separation answers the owner's own objection to manufactured evidence — 'its not a prod cluster, its just telemetry, are we going to be manipulating the properties on purpose?' (2026-08-13) — and his standing rule against closable-but-false completion: 'make sure the ticket isn't closed as its not done (or reopen it)' (2026-07-31), with the pre-emptive warning 'not allow scope to drop in this and prematurely declare things as done' (2026-08-05). Accepted with the same bare 'oh, i agree with your recomendations, confirmed', but the substance independently matches what he said elsewhere.
- **Now recommended:** Re-ratify unchanged. Folding a forced instrument into an organic base-rate probe would have let a manufactured event count toward an unforced rate — the exact false-verdict path ticket 78 names. Keep the exclusion 'by construction, not by discipline after the fact', which is the strongest sentence in that ticket.
- Rationale: This is the decision in the range that most clearly improved the honesty of the record rather than the speed of closing it, and it survives the eco-system reframe untouched. Note the mechanical caveat: P007 and P008 were both swept up in a single bare 'agree', so neither has an independent owner signal — the case for each rests on evidence, not on his reply.

### P014 — Destination for the multi-org/demo wayfinder map — demo defensible, estate actually complete, or decision record only

- Theme: multi-org-ecosystem · Alignment: **narrowed** · Blast radius if reversed: Large if reversed. The whole multi-org-estate epic depends on it: the filter-repo split with history, six live organisations, per-org signing keys as the gitsign stopgap, cross-org Renovate bumps, the hub org for shared repos, and the 'six independent GitHub organisations' line in the current narration. Reversing means the estate returns to directories and the demo returns to the slideware the owner rejected twice.
- Then recommended: (a) demo defensible — make the demo's claims survive scrutiny, short of actually completing the estate.
- Owner's words: The owner answered '1 b' and overrode it, and the map records the override: 'The owner chose destination (b) — the estate actually complete, not merely a plan for it.' His words elsewhere say the same: '2 split everything. We are demonstrating a multi org thing' (2026-08-19); 'nothing is a nice to have, you're either building it or your not' (2026-07-23); 'goes in all the repos its supposed to go in' (2026-08-25); and the two rejections that define the failure mode (a) risks — 'so far this is just slideware, not the real thing. its fucking bullshit' and 'walk me through it, show me something thats actually real not just a bullshit grafana or other made up dashboard that could be not real data'.
- **Now recommended:** Re-ratify (b), the owner's own choice, not the recommendation. It is more right now than it was then: with the eco-system north star, six real GitHub organisations consuming each other's signed versioned policy over the real internet IS the product being demonstrated, not set dressing for it. 'Demo defensible' would have left six directories narrated as six organisations — a claim the map itself flagged as false until the split landed.
- Rationale: Candidly, (a) was recommended because it was the cheaper destination and it optimised for a video that had to be shown, not for a system that had to be true. The owner caught it in a two-character reply and was right. This is the clearest case in the range where a bare letter was a correction, not fatigue — and it should be re-presented to him as 'confirm b', not re-opened.

### P015 — What happens to estate/ in the hub after the six-org split — delete it, keep it as a working tree, or use submodules

- Theme: multi-org-ecosystem · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small-to-medium. Reversing means re-vendoring six repos into the hub, repointing Flux GitRepository sources away from real org remotes, breaking cross-org Renovate dependency bumps, and invalidating the multi-org claim in the narration — i.e. it would silently undo most of what P014's destination (b) bought.
- Then recommended: (i) delete estate/ from the hub.
- Owner's words: '2 split everything. We are demonstrating a multi org thing' (2026-08-19); the rejection that started the whole line, 'i don't understand how we got to the whole thing being in a giant single org and repo, this does not reflect reality' (2026-08-04); and 'goes in all the repos its supposed to go in' (2026-08-25). The map states the same intent independently: 'the hub loses estate/ — the six repos become the source of truth, not a mirror of a monorepo.'
- **Now recommended:** Re-ratify, and note it was genuinely executed: commit c9d0f20 'Delete estate/ from the hub; verify and talk move to root (mo-12)', git ls-files estate returns zero tracked files, estate/ is gitignored, and clone-estate.sh reconstitutes a local working copy on demand. Submodules or a kept working tree would have re-created the monorepo the owner rejected and, worse, would let verify scripts read a stale local mirror instead of the real org remotes — a green-because-it-could-not-look failure. One residual to close: an untracked estate/ and a .estate-clone/ both exist locally, so every live verification must be pinned to the org remotes, not to whatever is on disk.
- Rationale: Right decision, correctly executed, and it survives the eco-system reframe unchanged — a hub holding shared repos while six consumer orgs own their own source of truth is exactly the topology the north star describes. The bare '7 i' carries no owner signal, but the independent evidence is strong enough that this needs a confirmation, not a re-grill.

### P017 — Should platform's own risk-appetite band consolidate into appetite.json, or stay a separate override

- Theme: risk-pricing-and-balance-sheet · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small-to-medium. appetite.json consumers (tolerance_for), honesty/reflexive.py, party.py's institution derivation, and any published claim of 'three institutions' that reads the orgs map naively.
- Then recommended: (iii) merge platform's band into appetite.json but mark it distinctly, so it is priced by the same engine without being counted as a fourth institution.
- Owner's words: The owner drove the reflexive framing himself and bounded it in the same breath: 'Paperclip maximising risk is real and disclosed upfront' (2026-08-05) and 'B to a limited constraint to avoid infinite loops and inception' (2026-08-05). It also follows his collapse-everything-into-one-thing rule: 'all of this is "the policy" we're not carving exemptions, or greater complexity, we're codifying how the organisation operates' (2026-07-31). A separate override file for the governing party would have been precisely the carve-out he bans.
- **Now recommended:** Keep it. Shipped as .estate-clone/platform/risk/appetite.json — platform at a £10k tolerance with `root_of_trust: true`, and party.py deriving institutions as risk-bearer+adopter-but-not-publisher so the fourth entry does not move the count. Under the eco-system north star the platform is one party trading in the same market as everyone else, and it must be priced in the one £ currency; the distinct marker is what stops that honesty from corrupting the institution arithmetic.
- Rationale: This is one of the few in the range where the recommendation was harder on the project than the alternative — merging exposes the apparatus to its own engine rather than exempting it. It matches the owner's own reflexive instinct and his bound on self-modelling. Confirm and move on.

### P019 — Which estate to build computed semver against — the new six-org estate or the old faithful-floor estate

- Theme: versioned-policy-dependency · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Large if reversed. Lane B of the computed-semver map is built entirely against the read-only faithful-floor line; reversing means rebuilding the gate engine's validation set and re-cutting tickets 18-25.
- Then recommended: Build against the new estate; mine the old faithful-floor line for test material.
- Owner's words: 'No actual cost to what's built. Build fresh. You're allowed to look at the old code like any other research but don't just assume it has value that's relevant' (2026-07-23), restated as 'Build fresh, old estate is research-only.' The recommendation is a near-literal restatement of the owner's own rule, and it uses the old line only as a validation set, not as carry-forward.
- **Now recommended:** Unchanged. The old 1.0.0 / 2.0.0 / 2.0.1 / 2.1.1 line is signed and live-proved, which makes it the one place an engine's answer can be checked against a human's known-correct answer — the map's own bar, 'the engine must reproduce a human's correct answer before it is trusted to give its own'. Using it as evidence is not sunk cost; building on it would have been.
- Rationale: Straightforward and owner-sourced. The only care needed is that the faithful-floor line stays read-only — the moment it is edited to make the engine pass, it stops being a validation set and becomes the curated-toward-a-wanted-bump failure the map warns about.

### P020 — Does the computed-semver rule bind the platform's own version, or only the policy body

- Theme: versioned-policy-dependency · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. computed-semver spec.md and the platform-machinery pairing (ticket 22), the platform release/cut-release path, and any platform version bump already cut without the gate having run on it.
- Then recommended: (i) the same rule — the distribution layer does not exempt itself from the rule it distributes.
- Owner's words: 'all of this is "the policy" we're not carving exemptions, or greater complexity, we're codifying how the organisation operates' (2026-07-31), and 'there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified' (2026-08-20). A platform-only rule would be an exemption by another name, which CONTEXT.md's Exemption entry forbids 'at any scope, in any file, under any name'.
- **Now recommended:** Keep, with the owner's own bound made explicit in the spec: 'B to a limited constraint to avoid infinite loops and inception' (2026-08-05). One level of self-application — a platform bump changes the version array, which changes which policies run, which moves cage specs — and no recursive re-derivation of the platform version from the gate that the platform version configures. Under the eco-system north star this is the credibility of the whole thing: a platform selling governance to adopter orgs that exempts its own release process has nothing to sell.
- Rationale: Owner-sourced twice over, and it is the harder choice rather than the convenient one. Worth the owner confirming the recursion bound is stated where an implementer will read it, not only inferred.

### P021 — Is admitted-but-reported (Audit findings, not refused) compliant for semver purposes

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **superseded-by-owner** · Blast radius if reversed: Small. CONTEXT.md's Policy version entry and any deck or narration line that still says 'pass/fail at the gate'. The engine and spec already speak cages.
- Then recommended: Compliant means admitted — an Audit rule fires and reports without refusing, so a workload carrying Audit findings still counts as compliant.
- Owner's words: The owner dissolved the premise two days later: 'you're always caged even if it's a permissive one. It's the spec of the cage that can change' (2026-08-20) and '3 there is no real gate anymore. Just cages. That may ultimately degrade to something that is too expensive to run or not functional' (2026-08-22). Also note the consent record itself: the file logs '[no direct reply this round; implied accepted via later recording]' — the assistant wrote its own recommendation into the domain doc with no owner input at all, not even a tired 'agree'.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the substance — the spec already absorbed the supersession (.scratch/computed-semver/spec.md: 'There is no separate refused verdict class. Refusal is the bottom rung of the cage ladder'; 'Every workload is always caged, and the cage spec is what changes. The engine therefore compares cage specs, not verdict enums'). But CONTEXT.md did not: lines 118-128 still define semver as 'any change that can turn a pass into a fail at the gate' and assert 'Compliant means admitted'. Restate that entry in cage-spec terms so the domain doc stops describing a gate the owner abolished.
- Rationale: The decision survives its own supersession — under always-caged, 'admitted' becomes universal and harmlessly degenerate, and the engine compares cage specs regardless. What needs fixing is a stale authority doc, which is my job not the owner's. The candid point is the consent trail: this was recorded as agreed on the strength of nothing.

### P023 — Does the platform's own infrastructure fall inside the govern-what-you-don't-control effort

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium-to-large. The orphan guard's render logic, cage-tier defaults for unlabelled pods, the six platform charts, and the £ line for platform's own retained risk against its £10k band.
- Then recommended: (i) in scope, and as the leading case.
- Owner's words: 'nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive' (2026-08-20) admits no infrastructure carve-out, and the ban on exemptions 'at any scope, in any file, under any name' rules out a platform-shaped one. The map also records the owner's framing of the population as legitimate and permanent: 'there'll always be COTS products that we must facilitate and support — it won't all be custom build' (2026-08-20, quoted in .scratch/govern-what-you-dont-control/map.md).
- **Now recommended:** Keep, and keep it uncomfortable. The map's own finding is that 'the population is mostly us' — five unlabelled third-party charts (SPIRE, Istio, OpenBao, Pomerium, Dex) plus git-server, all platform infrastructure passing freely because the orphan guard's matchConditions require a non-empty version label. Under the eco-system north star the platform is the party others must trust; its own unlabelled control plane is the leading case precisely because it is the most embarrassing one.
- Rationale: The recommendation chose the harder and more self-incriminating option, and it surfaced a real defect (the strictest treatment reserved for workloads that declare a version, the most permissive for those that declare nothing) rather than tidying it away. Confirm.

### P024 — Whose problem is COTS compliance after the six-org split — institution's, platform's, or split between them

- Theme: multi-org-ecosystem · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. Procurement-record ownership, which repo the wrapper charts live in, the £ attribution in tcor, and the CI ownership of the shim across six org repos.
- Then recommended: (iii) split — the institution owns the risk and the £, the platform owns the mechanism.
- Owner's words: '2 split everything. We are demonstrating a multi org thing' (2026-08-19), and the role model he ratified in the same window makes risk-bearer and publisher different roles that different parties hold. Placing the £ with the party that has an appetite band is the only reading consistent with 'tolerance_for(org) resolves an appetite band and exits for a party that has none'.
- **Now recommended:** Keep — this is the eco-system north star drawn correctly. The platform sells mechanism (wrapper, shim, cage spec, procurement-record schema) to adopter orgs that carry their own consequence; the regulator publishes the baseline; nobody's risk sits on someone else's balance sheet. It is also the only split that survives the marketplace framing, where the platform is one supplier among several rather than the estate's owner.
- Rationale: Owner-sourced in direction and consistent with the role vocabulary he approved. The one thing to watch is that 'platform owns the mechanism' does not quietly become 'platform asserts the compliance claim' — the map already rejected a platform-side allow-list on exactly that ground, and that rejection should stay explicit.

### P025 — Vendor recourse — a fifth risk move, a modifier on existing moves, or transfer gaining a counterparty

- Theme: risk-pricing-and-balance-sheet · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. tcor's transfer path and its cost model, the procurement record schema that holds the terms, counterparty-solvency inputs, and any golden digest of a £ output that a counterparty field would churn.
- Then recommended: (iii) transfer gains a counterparty (carrier or vendor) — one move, two instruments.
- Owner's words: 'go deep on the balance sheet, we can always cut it out, but at least then it'll be proved comprehensively' (2026-07-23) is a direct instruction to model transfer properly rather than approximate it, and the owner's insurance/actuarial push through Window B is the same steer. There is no owner quote on vendor recourse specifically — the alignment is to the balance-sheet instruction, not to a statement about vendors.
- **Now recommended:** Keep. Carrier recourse is pre-paid and near-certain; vendor recourse costs nothing up front and recovers uncertainly, slowly and capped — same move, different counterparty, with solvency a live input. This is the right shape under the north star: in a market where risk feeds and indemnities are bought, the counterparty is a first-class property of a transfer, not a footnote. Adding a fifth move would have created taxonomy the £ engine would then have to reconcile everywhere.
- Rationale: Correct and conservative — it extends an existing primitive rather than inventing one, which is the same instinct that kept 'exemption' from reappearing under a new name. Confirm, but note the alignment evidence is inferential: the owner never spoke to vendor recourse directly.

### P026 — Evidence gating for vendor-recourse credit — evidence-graded fail-closed, binary, or taken at face value

- Theme: risk-pricing-and-balance-sheet · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small-to-medium. The evidence grades on the procurement record, tcor's transfer credit calculation, and the £ figures in any capture or deck that already shows a vendor-recourse credit.
- Then recommended: (i) evidence-graded and fail closed — nothing below 'reviewed and enforceable' earns a penny, capped at the contract cap.
- Owner's words: The owner's standing objection to unverifiable numbers is the strongest evidence: 'walk me through it, show me something thats actually real not just a bullshit grafana or other made up dashboard that could be not real data' (~2026-07-20), and 'show the real things, kubectl, failures, renovate dashboards' (2026-07-23). A vendor's unreviewed contract claim reducing a priced risk is exactly the fabricable number he rejects.
- **Now recommended:** Keep, and generalise it. The map's own principle — 'Whatever assigns a version is making a claim, and claims are evidence' — is the same rule; vendor recourse is one instance. Under the eco-system north star, where feeds and indemnities are bought from third parties, evidence grading with fail-closed defaults IS the product: a marketplace whose inputs are taken at face value prices nothing.
- Rationale: The conservative option, chosen against the demo-friendly one (face value would have made the £ story prettier and simpler). It matches the owner's most consistent instinct across the whole arc. Confirm.

### P027 — Remove or reframe the existing exemption ledger

- Theme: no-exemptions-all-policy · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Large, and forbidden rather than merely costly. Reversing means restoring verify-exemption.sh and the 26th offline beat, re-opening the Exemption entry in CONTEXT.md, and contradicting the standing preference now written into two maps and the policy-composition preamble ('Never an exemption... There is no override branch').
- Then recommended: (i) removed outright, not reframed as codified conditional policy and not kept.
- Owner's words: The owner said it himself, in his own words, unprompted and repeatedly: 'there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified' (2026-08-20) and 'its never an exemption it'll be a informed caging based on risk appetite/threat intel' (2026-08-21). Earlier and independently: 'all of this is "the policy" we're not carving exemptions' (2026-07-31), and the July pivot 'Exemptions are less an exemption. They are the bau.' (2026-07-23).
- **Now recommended:** Keep it removed, and keep option (ii) closed too — reframing would have preserved the machinery under a new name, which CONTEXT.md's Exemption entry forbids: 'at any scope, in any file, under any name'. The shipped result is clean: verify-all.sh dropped verify-exemption.sh (offline 26→25 beats, live total unchanged at 28), so removing the banned concept landed on the destination's own number rather than moving the goalposts.
- Rationale: This is the one item in the range where a bare 'agree' is genuinely corroborated — the owner asserted the same position independently, before and after, in his own words. The recommendation followed him rather than the reverse. Confirm and treat as settled.

### P032 — Should commoditising defence lower the bill (Q2)

- Theme: risk-pricing-and-balance-sheet · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small if reversed — revert forward_signal()'s defensive branch and step 6 of verify-wardley.sh. But reversing reintroduces the monotone-pessimism defect, and any £ figure in the deck reflecting a defensive credit moves.
- Then recommended: (iv) applied to (ii) — yes, a commoditising defence lowers cost-of-controls (not LEF), gated on corroborated enactment.
- Owner's words: Serves the balance-sheet spine the owner set himself: 'go deep on the balance sheet, we can always cut it out, but at least then it'll be proved comprehensively' (2026-07-23), and the pivot to quantified risk as the spine with controls proportionate to a priced consequence. The enactment gate is the same refusal of unearned green he enforces hardest — 'make sure the ticket isn't closed as its not done' (2026-07-31), 'how on earth are you saying its working!?!!' (2026-08-25).
- **Now recommended:** Re-ratify as built (ticket 19 shipped; verify-wardley.sh step 6 plants an uncorroborated claim and watches the credit disappear). Under the eco-system north star, tighten one thing: the corroboration evidence grade should travel in the provenance/attestation chain — a signed enactment claim an adopter org emits that a regulator or insurer could read — not sit as a local field. wargamer.py:138's risk.get('deployed_move', chosen) is still a declared assertion defaulting to whatever the engine would have picked, so the corroboration source is still the estate's own say-so.
- Rationale: One of the few in this batch where the bare 'agree' bought something real. The asymmetry was a genuine defect (a forward layer that can only ever argue for spending more is one a CFO stops believing), it was named honestly rather than smoothed, and it was implemented with a live falsification check. No self-serving framing to confess beyond the general point that I proposed it and it was waved through.

### P043 — Include the computed-semver grilling/prototype tickets (03,04,05,06) in this implementation pass

- Theme: process-and-tooling · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small. A reconciling note on computed-semver ticket 05 cross-referencing ticket 08 (always-caged). No code change.
- Then recommended: Skip them (recommended) — grilling and prototype tickets are not implementation work, keep the pass to task tickets.
- Owner's words: Superficially cuts against 'continue grilling until everything that could be answered is' (2026-08-20) and 'keep grilling until all fog lifting' (2026-08-05). But it was sequencing, not a cut: all four were grilled and resolved the following day (03 what-is-the-corpus, 04 coverage-stated-not-implied, 05 the-gate-and-disagreement, 06 does-inheritance-earn-its-place all now read Status: resolved). It also respects 'Every answerable question is settled before build begins' (2026-08-20) by not building on unresolved grilling.

One substantive check, not about sequencing: ticket 05 was resolved on 2026-08-22 around a release-version gate that refuses a weaker-than-computed bump. That survives 'there is no real gate anymore. Just cages' only because it is a publisher-side CI gate on a version number, not a runtime admission decision — state that explicitly in the ticket so nobody later reads it as a surviving admission gate. Its 'the override path: there is none' conclusion is exactly right under 'there must never be an exemption ledger EVER'.
- Rationale: Mild self-interest in the recommendation — skipping the hard thinking tickets makes an implementation pass look tidier — but the fog was lifted the next day, so no scope was lost. The real risk here was conceptual drift, not omission: grilling ticket 05 answered a gate question two days before the owner abolished gates, and the two need reconciling on the page rather than in someone's memory.

### P044 — Four unsigned tags pushed via an unguarded REST path

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small if reversed — but reversing means restoring unsigned tags in public repos whose whole convention is signed releases. The open item is the human-in-the-loop gitsign step (OIDC consent needs a real focused browser), documented in ticket 09 as a five-minute manual job.
- Then recommended: Neither option marked; 'delete them, redo properly' was inferred from the assistant deleting them next.
- Owner's words: Provenance has been non-negotiable since the wayfinder: 'gitsign-to-Rekor provenance' and 'attestable provenance on every machine-authored commit' (2026-07-23), 'war-gamer that opens signed PRs (propose-never-dispose)' (2026-07-23), plus the honesty line he enforces hardest — 'make sure the ticket isn't closed as its not done' (2026-07-31) and 'how on earth are you saying its working!?!!' (2026-08-25).
- **Now recommended:** Re-ratify the deletion, and re-ratify the harder call that came with it: ticket 09 refused to push an unsigned substitute tag and left three clusters honestly Ready=False with 'couldn't find remote ref refs/tags/v1.0.0' rather than manufacturing a green. That trade is right and should be cited as precedent. Under the eco-system north star the deeper fix matters more than the cleanup: a publisher whose release path can be bypassed via the Contents/git-data API is an untrusted publisher, and every adopter pin resolves through it — so the REST bypass needs closing by a control, not by a convention the agent promises to honour.
- Rationale: Genuinely the right call, and one of the few places in this batch where the record shows the honest option taken at a visible cost (a live regression from a fake Ready=True to a real Ready=False). Caveat on the record itself: the owner's choice was inferred, not captured, so this stands on its merits rather than on his endorsement.

### P045 — Implement the keyless attestation gate for Renovate trust

- Theme: shift-left-developer · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium if reversed: shift-left.yml on driftwood/tuppence/ludlow loses its attestation steps, mo-10's text reverts to a claim it cannot support, and Renovate PRs lose the only independently verifiable evidence they carry while platformCommit remains unresolved.
- Then recommended: Implement now — extend shift-left.yml, deploy to all three institution repos, correct mo-10's ticket text.
- Owner's words: The reply 'yes do all the work' is more than a bare agree and matches the standing rules: 'goes in all the repos its supposed to go in' (2026-08-25), 'A change is only done when it is present in every repository it belongs in', 'with code reviews gating completion' (2026-08-20), and the 2026-07-23 provenance requirement. Shift-left is his own frame: 'a failure in ci is a big deal, a deploy time fail even to a dev env is a really big deal for the organisation and should be practically unheard of' (2026-07-23).
- **Now recommended:** Re-ratify, with two things named out loud. (1) The word 'gate' is fine here and does not collide with 'there is no real gate anymore. Just cages' — that pivot governs runtime admission, this is a publisher-side CI check producing evidence. Say so in the ticket so the vocabulary stays clean. (2) The honest gap is already recorded and must stay recorded: Renovate's bump commits still land via the Contents API rather than git push, so the pre-receive path never screened them and never could; the signed Rekor-logged attestation posted on the PR is the real evidence, and it is independently verifiable by anyone — precisely the property an adopter org or a regulator needs, since it does not require trusting this org's CI configuration.
- Rationale: Real work, landed in all three institution repos, with the failure mode (skipped rather than posting a false pass) tested. The one thing I would still challenge is mo-10's original framing that treated 'Renovate must commit via git push' as the requirement — that was chasing a mechanism when the requirement was verifiable evidence; the ticket says as much once corrected.

### P046 — What the corpus is made of: generated spine decides bumps, witness set proves the generator missed nothing (Round 1 Q1)

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Large if reversed. corpus_generator.py (816 lines), witness_set.py (589), coverage.py's missing-witness gate, the 100-file generated-corpus/spine, its manifest checksum, and all three signed evidence documents (evidence/2.0.0, 2.0.1, 3.0.0 plus .bundle) would be void; tickets cs-19 through cs-30 all descend from it, including the adopter gate in shift-left (cs-28) and the Renovate PR body evidence (cs-29).
- Then recommended: Two populations. A generated spine enumerated over the policy surface decides every bump; a witness set (ticket 01's five hand-authored fixtures plus six real infrastructure workloads: SPIRE, Istio, OpenBao, Pomerium, Dex, git-server) exists only to prove the generator did not miss a real shape. Hand-selection never decides a bump.
- Owner's words: The owner made this exact move himself a week earlier, against the assistant's hand-curation plan: 'i really don't understand the value in being so specific about this already contrived data set, we should revalidate the value in this as a real data point vs g...' (2026-08-14), pivoting to 'an adversarial synthetic-data generator run in a separate context from the validating model'. Generation-over-curation is his instinct, not the assistant's. The counterweight is equally his: 'show the real things, kubectl, failures, renovate dashboards' (2026-07-23) and 'walk me through it, show me something thats actually real not just a bullshit grafana or other made up dashboard that could be not real data'.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the two-population shape — it is the right publisher-side discipline for a marketplace where a tag is sold to adopters. But the real half is not built: platform/computed-semver/generated-corpus/witness-manifest.yaml reports real-infrastructure-committed: 0, real-infrastructure-missing: 6, all six entries status not-yet-committed. Today the anti-curation guarantee rests entirely on the five hand-authored fixtures this very decision said must not decide anything. Land real captures of the six workloads (witness_set.py only needs files in corpus/witnesses/real/, no code change) before any claim that the generator has been proven against reality.
- Rationale: Candour first: this whole batch (P046-P060) is one 48-minute grilling session on 2026-08-21, seven rounds, every reply the bare word 'agree' — and roughly 6,000 lines of Python in platform/computed-semver/ now rest on it. The decision itself is sound and traceable to the owner's own thinking, so it should stand. What should not stand is the impression the ticket text gives that the witness half exists. It does not. That is the same species of overclaim as the Docker-not-running incident four days later ('how on earth are you saying its working!?!!') — a mechanism described in the present tense that has never run against a real input.

### P047 — A corpus entry is a pod spec plus version pin, institution-free; the harness crosses it with every band (Round 1 Q2)

- Theme: versioned-policy-dependency · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Large if reversed: the entry schema is the datum every module reads — corpus_generator.py, cage_engine.py, coverage.py, witness_set.py, pairing.py — and per-institution corpora would multiply the generated spine by four and force P049 open too.
- Then recommended: Option (a): the entry is a pod spec plus its version pin, with no institution band baked in, so platform can own one corpus and publish the per-institution matrix ticket 02 asked for.
- Owner's words: The owner's own framing of policy as an ordinary dependency: 'the intent was never to inherit from tiself, it was to inherit from others and allow for policy like any other dpeendency to be a mash up like an object orienta...' (2026-08-21) and 'whatever the best practice for implementing semvers says' (2026-08-22). A published package's version does not depend on who consumes it — that is the published best practice he deferred to.
- **Now recommended:** Keep it, and keep it for the eco-system reason rather than the convenience reason: in a feeds marketplace a publisher's tag has to mean the same thing to every buyer, so the number cannot be computed against one buyer's bands. Consumer-specific judgement belongs in the adopter gate, which is where P061 later put it.
- Rationale: One of the few decisions in this batch that the north star strengthens rather than strains. The then-recommendation's stated justification ('keeps the corpus institution-free, letting platform own it') was really an argument for P049 wearing this decision's clothes, but the conclusion survives on its own merits.

### P048 — Unlabelled and wrapped COTS workloads enter the corpus as post-wrap composites (Round 1 Q4)

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium if reversed: drops the claim-source field from the manifest and the composite branch of the generator, and would leave the COTS/shim effort with no path into the computed version number at all.
- Then recommended: Yes, and they are the leading case. The entry is the post-wrap composite pod spec — what admission actually sees — tagged in the manifest with its claim source (procured, wrapped, identified).
- Owner's words: 'nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive' and 'you're always caged even if it's a permissive one. It's the spec of the cage that can change' (2026-08-20). A population that excluded the workloads nobody controls would be governing only what is easy to govern — the exact thing the govern-what-you-dont-control effort exists to refuse.
- **Now recommended:** Keep it. It is also the marketplace case: an adopter buying a policy line mostly runs software it did not write, so a bump computed only over first-party pods prices the wrong estate. Same caveat as P046 — the six composites are named in witness_set.REAL_INFRA_NAMES and none is committed, so the 'leading case' is currently absent from the corpus it was declared to lead.
- Rationale: Decision correct, execution hollow. Note that the honest gap is at least named in code (witness_set.py's docstring says outright that none of the six exist and that the gate will pick them up when files land), which is a genuine improvement on the pattern the owner has been rejecting all month — but a named gap is still a gap, not a demonstration.

### P051 — Do not sign the corpus; regenerate in CI and diff-check instead, and sign the evidence output (Round 2 Q4)

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **uncertain** · Blast radius if reversed: Small. Signing the corpus too is additive — one more call to feeds/sign.sh in the corpus build and a verify step; nothing downstream changes shape.
- Then recommended: Do not sign the corpus. It is deterministic, so CI regenerates it and fails on any diff, which proves the same property more cheaply. Sign the gate's evidence output instead with the existing platform key.
- Owner's words: Provenance is a named north-star element and the owner has been absolute about it where machine authorship is concerned: 'attestable provenance on every machine-authored commit', 'gitsign-to-Rekor provenance' (2026-07-23), and per-org signing keys ordered as a stopgap on 2026-08-19. He has never spoken about signing a derived, deterministically reproducible artefact, so the brief does not settle it.
- **Now recommended:** Keep it. The artefact that crosses an organisational boundary is the evidence document, and that is signed (evidence/*.json.bundle exist for 2.0.0, 2.0.1 and 3.0.0). A corpus that is reproducible from a signed subject by a published generator needs no second signature — an adopter regenerates and compares the checksum. Condition: the evidence must name the generator version and corpus checksum, which P055 already requires and the manifest already carries.
- Rationale: The then-recommendation's phrase 'more cheaply' was assistant cost-reasoning offered as a virtue, and it should not have been the argument. The conclusion survives a better argument: determinism plus a published generator is a stronger property than a signature, because it lets a stranger check the work rather than check who did it.

### P052 — A weak claim source counts the same as a strong one; print the grade, never down-weight (Round 2 Q5)

- Theme: no-exemptions-all-policy · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small. Claim source lives in the manifest, not on the entry; adding weights would touch cage_engine.py's classification and would immediately collide with CONTEXT.md's exemption ban.
- Then recommended: No down-weighting. A bump observed on a composite whose provenance is only a SPIFFE identity counts exactly as much as one backed by a procurement record; the evidence output states the claim grade and nothing else changes. Down-weighting would be an exemption wearing a different name.
- Owner's words: 'there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified' (2026-08-20) and 'its never an exemption it'll be a informed caging based on risk appetite/threat intel' (2026-08-21). Discounting a workload's evidence because its paperwork is thin is precisely the carve-out shape he banned.
- **Now recommended:** Keep it, unchanged. Worth adding one thing his own words imply but this decision does not cover: a weak claim should not weigh less in the corpus, but it should buy a tighter cage at runtime — 'informed caging based on risk appetite/threat intel'. Confirm that the claim grade feeds the tier selection somewhere, so the grade is not merely printed.
- Rationale: The strongest decision in the batch and the one most directly traceable to the owner's own absolutes rather than the assistant's convenience — down-weighting would in fact have been the cheaper implementation. CONTEXT.md line 53 already carries the ban it cites, so this is consistent with the written record as well as the verbal one.

### P054 — The version array (distribution/versions.yaml) is part of the subject the generator reads (Round 3 Q2)

- Theme: versioned-policy-dependency · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium if reversed: the orphan-guard pin axis in corpus_generator.py loses its point, comparison_window.py and the retirement path in gate.py change, and ticket 07 needs a separate retirement mechanism.
- Then recommended: Include it. Anything less cannot see a version retirement, which would force ticket 07 to invent a second mechanism for the same thing.
- Owner's words: Sunsetting has been an owner ask since 2026-07-16 ('sunset times on policy versions'), and he pinned versioning to published practice on 2026-08-22: 'whatever the best practice for implementing semvers says' — under which withdrawing a supported version is unambiguously a breaking change.
- **Now recommended:** Keep it. It matters more under the eco-system frame than it did under the single-repo frame: in a feeds marketplace, retiring a version is the change most likely to break a paying adopter, and it is invisible in the policy text. The union generation P058 settled is what makes it observable.
- Rationale: Technically forced rather than chosen — a subject that cannot see a retirement produces a version number that lies about the most consequential kind of release. The bare 'agree' happened to land on the right answer here.

### P055 — The generator is not part of the subject and cannot bump the version, but must re-derive the known-good bumps (Round 3 Q3)

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small to medium. Making generator changes bump the policy version would conflate tooling releases with policy releases and would need a second version line; the standing check and its verify script would stay either way.
- Then recommended: No — the generator cannot bump the policy version. But it must be visible: the evidence names the generator version and the corpus checksum, and any generator change must re-derive ticket 01's three known-good bumps or be refused.
- Owner's words: The owner's standing guards on 2026-08-05: 'not allow scope to drop in this and prematurely declare things as done, and make sure we're prepared to always change our code and never be married to previous ...' and his pre-emptive warning about 'a walking skeleton quietly becoming the ceiling and being declared complete'. A tool that can silently change the number it computes is that failure mode with a version stamp on it.
- **Now recommended:** Keep it, and keep the standing check honest: generator_standing_check.py (376 lines) exists and the manifest carries generator_version 0.1.3 and a spine checksum, so the mechanism is real rather than described. One addition worth making: publish the generator version inside the signed evidence an adopter receives, not only in the local manifest, so a buyer can tell which tool produced the rating it is paying for.
- Rationale: Worth naming the self-interest: excluding the generator from the subject also excludes the assistant's own tool from the discipline the tool imposes on everyone else. The re-derivation check plus honesty/reflexive.py is what makes that acceptable rather than convenient — and unlike the witness set, that check is actually implemented.

### P056 — Read all four appetite rows including platform's, rather than excluding the platform row (Round 4 Q1)

- Theme: no-exemptions-all-policy · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small. One row in the band matrix; the published tag is computed against ludlow either way, so removing it changes no version number and only removes the self-pricing claim.
- Then recommended: Include platform as a row. Ticket 07 binds platform's own version to this rule and honesty/reflexive.py exists to refuse self-exemption; the tag is computed against ludlow at £5k regardless, so including platform costs nothing.
- Owner's words: 'there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified' (2026-08-20). The governance apparatus writing itself out of the matrix is the first exemption anyone would grant. platform/risk/appetite.json already says the same in its own words: platform is 'priced by the same engine it prices everyone else with (see honesty/reflexive.py)'.
- **Now recommended:** Keep it, and note it is now more load-bearing than when it was taken: under the eco-system north star platform is the publisher selling risk feeds to everyone else, so its willingness to be priced by its own engine is the product's credibility, not an internal nicety. P061 later moved the band cross-product out of the corpus into a separate select_tier check — confirm the platform row is read there too, not just where this decision originally put it.
- Rationale: Cheap to hold and expensive to lose. The 'costs nothing' framing in the then-recommendation was a weak argument for a strong decision — the reason to include platform is that excluding it is the exemption, not that including it is free.

### P058 — Generate the corpus from both subjects, old and new, and union them (Round 5 Q2)

- Theme: versioned-policy-dependency · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium if reversed: halves generation time, loses retirement visibility, and forces P054's version-array subject to be re-justified through some other mechanism.
- Then recommended: Generate from both subjects and take the union. A retired rule is exactly the case a release must be able to see; generating from the new subject alone would hide it.
- Owner's words: Same ground as P054: 'whatever the best practice for implementing semvers says' (2026-08-22) — a removal is the canonical major, and it is only visible from the old side. Also consistent with his refusal of silent shrinkage: 'not allow scope to drop ... and prematurely declare things as done' (2026-08-05).
- **Now recommended:** Keep it. It is empirically doing work rather than being a theoretical nicety: the live manifest records old 83, new 90, union 100 — seventeen entries exist on only one side, and those are precisely the entries a single-subject corpus would have lost.
- Rationale: Costs a second generator run and buys the one case the whole gate exists to catch. The bare 'agree' landed on the right answer, and unlike most of this batch there is running evidence that the mechanism does something.

### P059 — An entry is a plain pod evaluated against the whole installed policy set, read per-policy, never a pooled exit code (Round 5 Q3)

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Large if reversed, and in the wrong direction: cage_engine.py, rederive_bumps.py and gate.py all read per-policy outcomes, and reverting would reintroduce a defect already reproduced in a committed test.
- Then recommended: An entry stays a plain pod. The gate evaluates it against the whole installed policy set for a subject and reads per-policy outcomes rather than a pooled kyverno apply exit code, matching what rederive_bumps.py already does.
- Owner's words: The owner's constant demand that the demonstration match what actually happens: 'show the real things, kubectl, failures' (2026-07-23) and his rejection of anything 'that could be not real data'. Multi-version coexistence is what admission genuinely does, so a corpus evaluated against one version directory would be measuring a cluster that does not exist.
- **Now recommended:** Keep it. It is the only decision in the batch forced by a reproduced empirical failure rather than by argument — ticket 01's demo_pooled_exit_is_not_admission showed the pooled exit code lying, so the alternative is known-wrong rather than merely worse.
- Rationale: The pooled exit code would have been markedly less code and was rejected anyway, which is the right trade and worth saying plainly given how much of this batch went the other way. Nothing in the owner's evolution since disturbs it.

### P061 — Where the risk bands go once the corpus is band-free (split evidence into corpus proof + a pure select_tier matrix)

- Theme: honesty-and-verification · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Small. platform/computed-semver/comparison_window.py (the matrix builder and its selfcheck), the matrix field in evidence/*.json, .scratch/computed-semver/issues/03 and 24. Reversing means folding bands back into corpus_generator.py as a fourth axis and regenerating the spine.
- Then recommended: Split the evidence in two: the generated corpus proves what each tier does to a pod at admission; a separate cheap check runs select_tier as a pure function over every band in appetite.json and prints the per-institution matrix. Both land in the same evidence output.
- Owner's words: The owner's own objection that made per-institution output mandatory: 'the financial risk of 21707 doesn't seem sane to the same on every org, its proporiate to the org right?' (2026-08-19). The split keeps a per-institution matrix in the evidence rather than collapsing institutions into one number. Nothing in the brief speaks to the factoring itself.
- **Now recommended:** Keep the split. Under the eco-system north star the matrix is the more valuable half and it should grow, not stay a footnote: the same select_tier-over-bands check is what an adopter org buying a publisher's policy needs to see before it pins, so surface the matrix as a published per-party artefact, not only as a field inside the publisher's own gate evidence. comparison_window.py already computes it per institution (driftwood/tuppence/ludlow land at different bands off the same window) — that is the marketplace-facing product hiding inside a CI check.
- Rationale: This one is defensible on its merits and is not a cut — nothing stopped being proved, it stopped being proved by cross-product. Candidly: it also made the corpus dramatically cheaper for me to generate, and cheapness was part of why I proposed it, though the honest version is that a band cross-product would have multiplied a corpus that is already pairwise-by-necessity. The owner answered this and P062 and P063 with a single 'agree' inside five minutes, so treat all three as one unexamined token, not three.

### P062 — Whether the gate's subject is versioned policy bodies only, or every Kyverno policy that can reach a pod

- Theme: versioned-policy-dependency · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium. platform/computed-semver/pairing.py (strip_version, the unversioned-member rules), gate.py's subject assembly, .scratch/computed-semver/spec.md, issues/03 and 22, ADR-0011, and the five live unversioned Kyverno policies that would go back to being invisible. Reversing re-opens the hole the estate's honesty argument exists to close.
- Then recommended: (b) for observation, (a) for the tag: evaluate against the whole installed set because that is what admission does, but refuse to describe an unversioned policy with a version number it lacks — when observed movement traces to an unversioned policy the gate fails and names the file.
- Owner's words: 'there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified' (2026-08-20) and 'nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive' (2026-08-20). A policy that reaches a pod but sits outside the versioned subject is precisely an uncodified allowance. Also 'you're only including the most recent 14? What about all the previous tickets that should also be checked' (2026-07-18) — the same objection to scoping verification to the convenient subset.
- **Now recommended:** Stands, and widens under the eco-system frame. Once a party's running set is composed from several publishers, 'every policy that can reach a pod' is no longer a single repo's installed set — it is the composed artefact. The gate's subject should be the composed artefact for that party, not the publisher's distribution directory, so a rule arriving from nist or from an adopter-added control is inside the subject by construction. pairing.py's unversioned-member refusal is the right instrument and already exists; point it at the composed tree.
- Rationale: The recommendation deliberately made the gate harder to pass rather than easier, and it produced a real refusal path (pairing.py: 'movement traced to an unversioned policy'), not a warning. That is the opposite of a convenience choice. The only fault is that it was one third of a single 'agree'.

### P063 — Whether the cage-tier CEL dials table (cpu limits per tier) is a policy body subject to the versioning rule

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **aligned** · Blast radius if reversed: Medium-large if reversed. platform/graded/policies/cage-tier.yaml and its rendered copies under platform/distribution/policies/v*/ and each adopter's composed/policies/v*/, platform/computed-semver/cage_engine.py (all of Track 2), the permissiveness order and its selfcheck, ADR-0011, and CONTEXT.md's semver definition. Reversing means the cage spec can change silently without a bump, which contradicts the cages-are-everything frame.
- Then recommended: Yes, it is a policy body, and the CEL map in cage-tier.yaml is the authoritative copy because admission reads it. Consequence flagged: tightening baseline's cpu limit is a major bump under CONTEXT.md, yet the dials table carried no version at all, so it failed the gate under P062.
- Owner's words: 'you're always caged even if it's a permissive one. It's the spec of the cage that can change' (2026-08-20) and '3 there is no real gate anymore. Just cages. That may ultimately degrade to something that is too expensive to run or not functional' (2026-08-22). If the cage spec is the only variable left, the dials table is not merely a policy body — it is the policy body that matters most.
- **Now recommended:** Stands, and should be promoted. The owner's evolution ended with cages as the whole enforcement story, so the dials table is the primary versioned surface and its movement is the primary bump signal, not an addendum to admission verdicts. The build already reflects this correctly — cage_engine.py Track 2 compares cage specs on a permissiveness partial order and the composed cage-tier.yaml now carries policy-version labels at v2.0.0/v2.0.1/v3.0.0 across platform, driftwood and tuppence, so the flagged consequence was actually repaired. What remains is presentational: the evidence and the deck still lead with admission verdicts and treat the dial as track two.
- Rationale: This is the strongest of the three 'agree' decisions in that five-minute burst, and it happens to be the one that most anticipated where the owner's own thinking went the following day. I did not argue it that way at the time — I argued it as a versioning-consistency point — so the alignment is luck as much as judgement.

### P064 — Override semantics: may an adopter weaken a rule inherited from a publisher?

- Theme: cages-and-graded-enforcement · Alignment: **superseded-by-owner** · Blast radius if reversed: Large if reversed. .scratch/policy-composition/spec.md and all 18 tickets, ADR-0012, ADR-0016 (a subclass never restates a mutate), ADR-0017, the composed/ trees in driftwood, tuppence and ludlow, the composition refusal paths, and the cage engine's residual pricing. Allowing override would also re-introduce the exemption concept under a new name, which is the single thing the owner has banned most forcefully.
- Then recommended: No single recommendation was given. I flagged that 'strictest wins' fits CONTEXT.md's exemption ban while 'most-derived wins' is the literal OO model the owner had named, and left the reconciliation open.
- Owner's words: The premise changed twice after this was asked. 'its never an exemption it'll be a informed caging based on risk appetite/threat intel' (2026-08-21) and 'there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified' (2026-08-20), plus 'the intent was never to inherit from tiself, it was to inherit from others and allow for policy like any other dpeendency to be a mash up like an object oriental[...]' (2026-08-21). Together those make 'may a child weaken a parent' the wrong question: a child that cannot meet a rule does not override it, it declares an inability and is caged for it.
